## 44. [How JWST is Revolutionizing Our View of Early Galaxy Formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18960) <a id="link44"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18960

**Authors:** Rychard Bouwens

**Abstract:** The James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) has been revolutionizing our understanding of early galaxy formation over its first four years of science operations. With un- precedented sensitivity, spatial resolution, and wavelength coverage from the near- to mid-infrared, JWST has enabled astronomers to identify hundreds of star-forming galaxies at redshifts z>~8 (within ~650 million years of the Big Bang) through strong spectral breaks and characterize these galaxies through sensitive spectroscopy. These capabilities have provided new insights into the stellar populations, neutral gas, nebular continuum emission, chemical enrichment, dust content, and ionization conditions of galaxies during cosmic dawn, revealing more numerous and brighter systems than anticipated from pre-JWST models. We discuss the implications of these discoveries for reionization, the buildup of stellar mass and metals, and the evolution of galaxy populations and luminosity functions. JWST is also revealing a new physical picture of early galaxies, characterized by compact, clumpy star formation, high gas fractions, and intense radiation fields. Despite much progress, many open questions remain, including the efficiency, intermittency, and stellar initial mass function (IMF) of early star formation, conditions in the interstellar medium (ISM), masses, and structure of the earliest galaxies, and the topology of reionization. Resolving these open questions will require lensing magnification, sensitive spectroscopy to > 5 micron, including with the Atacama Large Millimeter Array (ALMA), exploration to higher redshifts, and larger, more complete samples, helping to clarify how the first galaxies formed and evolved.

## 45. [A radio counterpart of the local little-red-dot J092359+415736](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17559) <a id="link45"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17559

**Authors:** Tao An, Ruqiu Lin, Ailing Wang

**Abstract:** Little red dots (LRDs) are compact red sources at z>~4. Broad lines suggest accreting black holes, yet radio and X-ray measurements are usually weak. Local LRDs allow multi-frequency, multi-epoch radio studies that are inaccessible at high redshift. We search archival radio images for a counterpart to the local LRD J092359+415736 at z~0.323, quantify its long-term radio variability, and identify the plausible origin of the radio emission. Flux densities at the optical position are measured in FIRST, LoTSS, RACS, VAST, and VLASS products spanning 0.144-3.0 GHz and 1994-2024. A compact 144 MHz counterpart is detected at 42sigma in LoTSS, coincident with the Gaia position, and is recovered in FIRST, RACS, VAST, and VLASS. LoTSS and ASKAP give a spectral index alpha ~ -0.30 +/- 0.11. The source varies on multiple timescales: FIRST at 1.4 GHz (1994) is a factor of ~2.6 below ASKAP (2021-2024) and below the extrapolated LoTSS-ASKAP spectrum, indicating long-term brightening over 26 yr; the 3 GHz flux density rises by 2.3sigma between VLASS epochs 2.2 and 3.2. A star-formation-only origin would require ~200 Msun/yr, against an unobscured H-alpha upper limit of 3.3 Msun/yr, implying an extreme obscured starburst. Ordinary radio supernovae and tidal disruption events are strongly disfavoured by the luminosity and 30-yr recovery, while refractive scintillation would itself require an ultracompact nuclear emitter. The persistent radio counterpart is best explained by accretion onto the central black hole. Among the three radio-detected local LRDs, this source is intermediate in luminosity and the only one with reported multi-year variability, adding a temporal dimension to the radio diversity of the class.

**Authors:** Robin Anthony-Petersen, Orion Ning, Yu Watanabe, Benjamin R. Safdi, Maíra Dutra, Francesca Calore, Savitri Gallego, William Guan, Christopher M. Karwin, Alexander Kusenko, Israel Martinez-Castellanos, Shigeki Matsumoto, Michela Negro, Uwe Oberlack, Field R. Rogers, Thomas Siegert, Tadayuki Takahashi, Haoyu Xie, Hiroki Yoneda, Andreas Zoglauer, Steven E. Boggs, Dieter H. Hartmann, Carolyn A. Kierans, John A. Tomsick

**Abstract:** The decay of dark matter (DM) is a generic prediction of many particle physics models, producing distinctive monochromatic signatures in environments such as the Galactic halo. Searches for decaying DM in the elusive 'MeV gap' have remained conspicuously under-explored, despite well-motivated models including axion-like particles, sterile neutrinos, and hidden-sector scenarios predicting signatures in this energy range. The upcoming Compton Spectrometer and Imager (COSI) telescope will help bridge this gap. Designed to probe this energy band with high spectral resolution, low background, and wide field of view, COSI is well-suited for DM line searches at the MeV scale. In this work, we make projections for COSI's 2-year sensitivity for DM decay in the Milky Way halo using dedicated all-sky simulations incorporating COSI's predicted instrument response. We conclude that COSI will improve the sensitivity to the DM lifetime by more than an order of magnitude over the DM mass range 0.4 to 10 MeV.

## 47. [A0538-66: A transient ultraluminous X-ray source with a relativistic outflow, 69ms pulsations, and anomalous optical activity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17982) <a id="link47"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17982

**Authors:** Lorenzo Ducci, Sandro Mereghetti, Ciro Pinto, Matteo Bachetti, Motoki Nakajima, Rahul Sharma, Stefano Covino, Michela Rigoselli, Caterina Tresoldi, Tatehiro Mihara, Mutsumi Sugizaki

**Abstract:** A0538-66 is a peculiar Be/X-ray binary in the Large Magellanic Cloud, hosting the fastest spinning neutron star of its class (~69ms) and showing the brightest optical bursts of any system of this type. We report four XMM-Newton observations obtained near periastron in 2025, together with MAXI and REM monitoring, and a reanalysis of archival RXTE data. On 30 September 2025 the source reached L_X~1.5E39 erg/s (0.2-10 keV), the highest level since 1980, and in February, May, and August 2026, MAXI detected similarly bright outbursts. During the XMM-Newton observation taken on 30 September 2025, we detected the pulsations, measuring P_spin=69.25878(2) ms and dP_spin/dt=6.58(9)E-10 s/s; the pulsed fraction was low (~3% in 1-10 keV), increased with energy, and the soft and hard pulse profiles were shifted by half a cycle. A new detection in RXTE data of 3 December 1998 gives a faster spin period of P_spin=68.88813(28) ms. The XMM-Newton/RGS spectra show emission lines from photoionised plasma at rest and blueshifted absorption lines that imply an outflow at ~0.12c. This indicates a highly super-Eddington accretion rate and an intrinsic L_X~1.5E39 erg/s, and it makes A0538-66 a transient ultra-luminous X-ray pulsar, a class of which only a few objects are known so far. Since 2023 the optical bursts observed by REM have become longer and brighter, resembling those of the 1980s, but most of them lack a luminous X-ray counterpart (L_X>1E37 erg/s). We interpret these results in terms of a radiation pressure dominated accretion disc whose geometrically thick inner regions intermittently screen the innermost emitting regions.

## 48. [Tracing Accretion and Inner-Disk Gas Evolution in the Orion Nebula via Br-alpha and CO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19244) <a id="link48"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19244

**Authors:** Konstantin V. Getman, Maria Claudia Ramirez-Tannus, Jenny Frediani, Kevin L. Luhman, Eric D. Feigelson, Bayron Portilla-Revelo, Christian Rab, Valentin Brunn, Jan Forbrich, Thomas J. Haworth, Rens Waters, Arjan Bik, Charles J. Law, Andrew J. Winter, Vladimir S. Airapetian, Abygail R. Waggoner, Michael A. Kuhn, L. Ilsedore Cleeves, Sergio A. Dzib, Marco Padovani

**Abstract:** We present a systematic spectroscopic study of hydrogen Brackett-alpha (Br-alpha) and carbon monoxide (CO) fundamental-band (4.3-5.2 micron) emission from a homogeneous sample of 0.2-1.5 Msun pre-main-sequence (PMS) stars in the Orion Nebula observed with JWST/NIRSpec-MOS as part of our Chandra-JWST project. Using 73 Br-alpha and 46 CO spectra, we investigate correlations between warm-gas emission, stellar properties, accretion, and environment with censored correlation and regression analyses accounting for multiple testing. Br-alpha luminosity correlates strongly with accretion luminosity (Lacc), even after excluding sources within 2' of Theta1 Ori C, the nebula's main ionizing O-type star, whose radiation may bias proplyd Lacc estimates. This supports Br-alpha as a robust empirical tracer of accretion in PMS stars. Br-alpha also correlates with visual extinction (Av) and more weakly with stellar bolometric luminosity and mass. CO fundamental-band luminosities likewise correlate positively with Lacc and Av, relationships that persist in a larger combined JWST/NIRSpec+MIRI sample spanning multiple star-forming regions. In contrast to Br-alpha, CO luminosity shows no significant dependence on basic stellar properties, suggesting that warm molecular gas is governed primarily by accretion and local disk conditions. The weaker correlations of the Spitzer/IRAC spectral slope with Lacc and Av suggest that warm inner-disk gas may evolve or disperse faster than optically thick dust. Overall, the CO results tentatively indicate declining warm-gas emission as disks evolve, modulated by accretion-powered thermal and ultraviolet excitation, although variations in emitting area inherited from natal core conditions may also contribute.

## 49. [Estimating the spectral resolution of a radio data cube from its noise properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17223) <a id="link49"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17223

**Authors:** P. Serra, G. Mulas, K. M. Hess, P. Kamphuis, T. Westmeier, Y. Ascasibar, J. M. van der Hulst

**Abstract:** We present a new method to estimate the spectral resolution of a radio data cube from its noise properties. We assume that the data cube is the result of the convolution of a spectrally-uncorrelated cube with a constant convolution kernel K. We demonstrate that K and the resolution, defined as K's integral, can be estimated thanks to the analytical relation between the autocorrelation of the noise spectra extracted from the cube and the power spectrum of the kernel. We discuss a number of algorithms that make this method robust in the realistic case of a finite number of spectra and channels, real signal and artefacts, and we present the results obtained for a number of datasets from the literature. We further highlight that this method is applicable to any data cube that meets our assumptions independently of the observing frequency or the exact nature of the cube's "third" axis, e.g., it could be used to measure the time resolution of transient-search data cubes. We make this method available as part of the open-source software tool spectre.

## 50. [Testing the 4-Point Correlation Function as a Probe of Parity Violation with the Quijote-Odd Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18707) <a id="link50"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18707

**Authors:** William Ortolá Leonard, Zachary Slepian, Alex Krolewski, Simon May

**Abstract:** We investigate parity violation searches with the 4-Point Correlation Function (4PCF) using the Quijote and Quijote-Odd halo catalogs. Specifically, we explore the auto- and cross-correlation searching methodologies that have been previously used in the Baryon Oscillation Spectroscopic Survey (BOSS) and the Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI) searches. Although the Quijote-Odd simulations are initialized with a parity-violating primordial trispectrum, we find no statistically detectable parity-odd signal in the halo 4PCF. Instead, we find that the halo measurements are dominated by Poisson noise. We are able to distinguish this noise-dominated behavior from the response expected for a parity-odd signal. These results clarify how parity-sensitive 4PCF statistics respond to noise in a controlled simulation setting.

## 51. [Extinction by non-spherical particles with the Nanoparticle Transition Matrix Code: perspectives for interstellar medium and exoplanetary atmospheres](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17081) <a id="link51"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17081

**Authors:** G. La Mura, G. Mulas, M. A. Iatì, C. Cecchi-Pestellini, G. Aresu, S. Rezaei, R. Saija

**Abstract:** The Transition Matrix formalism (T-matrix) offers an optimal solution to the problem of radiation scattering and absorption by particles characterized by a high degree of chemical and structural complexity. However, its application to particle models composed by large numbers of constituents has been classically limited by the substantial computational burden of the method. As a consequence, the investigation of light scattering and absorption processes, in cases where particle asymmetry and non-homogeneity play an important role, typically need to rely on simplifying assumptions which affect the correct interpretation of transmission spectra and extinction curves. This problem has a huge impact on many fields of scientific application. In particular, it has a critical role in astrophysics, because the ability to model highly non homogeneous particles is a fundamental requirement to understand the role of extinction in different phases of the interstellar medium (ISM), as well as to probe the physics of light transmission in planetary atmospheres within the Solar System and beyond. In this work we describe the application of a new implementation of the T-matrix formalism, based on the framework originally developed by Borghese, Denti & Saija and named Nano-particle Transition Matrix code, which leverages the power of modern hardware architectures to reduce the computational costs related to the solution of relevant particle models for the investigation of dust in the ISM and of hot-Jupyter class planets atmospheric aerosols. This new implementation of the T-matrix formalism can handle calculations of increasing complexity with a direct impact in the interpretation of the transmission spectra of transiting exoplanets produced by the James Webb Space Telescope (JWST) and of those expected from the Atmospheric Remote-sensing Infrared Exoplanet Large survey (ARIEL).

**Novelty:** 5

## 52. [Intermittency in the Multiphase ISM --- II. Non-Gaussian Dust Polarization and Foreground Variance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17390) <a id="link52"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17390

**Authors:** Yue Hu

**Abstract:** Interstellar turbulence produces non-Gaussian polarized Galactic dust emission whose higher-order statistics depend on thermal phase and turbulent forcing. We synthesize optically thin dust polarization from three thermally multiphase diffuse interstellar medium (ISM) simulations with compressive, mixed, and solenoidal forcing. We characterize the resulting non-Gaussian structure in the warm, unstable, and cold phases using $E$- and $B$-mode spectra, one-point distributions, scale-dependent increments, and Minkowski functionals. Both $E$ and $B$ modes are intermittent. Warm-phase emission remains closest to Gaussian, whereas cold gas shows flatter intermediate-scale spectra, broader non-Gaussian tails, and the strongest departures from Gaussian morphology. Compressive forcing generally produces the strongest projected intermittency, particularly in the cold phase and total emission, while mixed and solenoidal forcing show weaker signatures. These higher-order structures also enhance spatial fluctuations in polarized dust power: Gaussian fields matched to the same two-point covariance underestimate the region-to-region scatter by factors of up to $\sim2.6$. Our results show that thermal phase and turbulent forcing regulate not only the mean polarized dust spectrum but also the field-to-field distribution of foreground power, with implications for modeling Galactic foreground in CMB polarization measurements.

## 53. [Blackbody evolution and circumstellar medium properties of broad-lined H-rich superluminous supernovae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16527) <a id="link53"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16527

**Authors:** Tuomas Kangas, Takashi Nagao, Uliana Pylypenko, Steve Schulze, Lin Yan

**Abstract:** Broad-lined H-rich superluminous supernovae (SLSNe II) tend to show signs of interaction, including a UV excess compared to a blackbody function. However, a quantitative analysis of the CSM configuration and the UV excess has so far not been performed for most of the literature sample. Additionally, SN 2023gpw showed signs of asymmetry in its peculiar blackbody and spectral evolution, raising the question of whether similar properties can be found in other SLSNe II. We aim to characterize the circumstellar environment of SLSNe II by searching for signs of asymmetry, quantifying the UV excess, and investigating any IR echoes produced by dust. Diversity among SLSNe II is also explored. We fit blackbody functions to the SEDs of SLSNe II to quantify the UV excess luminosity and its evolution. We use this to investigate mass-loss rates required to produce the excess through interaction. We also investigate the line profiles in SLSN II at late times and compare line velocities to the blackbody radius evolution. We examine public WISE IR images and place constraints on IR echoes. We find mass loss rates of $\sim10^{-4}$ to $\sim0.03\, \mathrm{M_\odot}$~yr$^{-1}$ when assuming all interaction luminosity emerges in the UV, i.e., conservative lower limits for the total mass loss rate. Radii calculated from line velocities in late-time spectra are mostly too large for a spherical interaction photosphere, only compatible with the blackbody radius evolution if the CSM is non-spherical with a low filling factor. This is in some cases supported by a mismatch between spectroscopic and blackbody expansion velocities. Considerable diversity is seen in the sample, probably as a result of different viewing angles and CSM masses. WISE images seem too shallow to observe IR echoes in SLSNe II beyond $z\sim0.1$, but in the nearest event, SN 2020jhm, a likely IR echo is detected, suggesting pre-existing dust.

## 54. [Black hole spin-mass correlation and accretion disks at birth stage of dense star clusters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18505) <a id="link54"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18505

**Authors:** Zacharias Roupas

**Abstract:** We review the accretion-driven channel of stellar black-hole growth and spin-up in gaseous star clusters, developed within the framework of our MSCA project "protoBH". Our detailed calculations involving semi-analytic modelling, dynamical and hydrodynamic simulations suggest that the growth and spin-up of initially low-mass stellar black holes is an integral element of the formation process of dense massive star clusters, such as the proto-globular clusters observed at high redshift. The black holes accrete residual gas, remaining from the first star formation event, and form accretion disks generated by gas angular momentum induced by the black-holes' own curved orbits. Assuming initial black hole masses below the pair-instability mass gap $\leq 55\,{\rm M}_{\odot}$, the gap gets populated with black-hole masses growing to $\sim 10^2\,{\rm M}_{\odot}$, and even reaching several hundred solar masses for sufficiently dense and massive proto-clusters. We predict a consistent spin-mass relation with low black hole spins $\lesssim 0.2$ for low black hole masses $\lesssim 20\,{\rm M}_{\odot}$, spanning all intermediate spins for higher masses and saturating to high spins $\gtrsim 0.8$ for high masses $\gtrsim 70\,{\rm M}_{\odot}$. We find evidence in favour of such an accretion-driven subpopulation of black holes in the GWTC-5.0 released by the LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA collaboration.

## 55. [Phase Separation of HI in the Draco Nebula: CNM formation Triggered by Colliding WNM Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17753) <a id="link55"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17753

**Authors:** Yuhi Naruse, Hiroaki Yamamoto, Yamato Matsuzuki, Rin Yamada, Atsushi Nishimura, Toshikazu Onishi

**Abstract:** We investigate the thermal phase structures and kinematics of atomic hydrogen (H I) gas in the Draco nebula to clarify how infalling H I evolves toward cold atomic and molecular clouds. Using the DHIGLS H I 21 cm data, we apply the Regularized Optimization for Hyper-Spectral Analysis (ROHSA) algorithm to decompose the emission into spatially coherent Gaussian components, and classify them as warm neutral medium (WNM), lukewarm neutral medium (LNM), and cold neutral medium (CNM). We identify two kinematically distinct WNM components: WNM1 near $-3$ km s$^{-1}$ and WNM2 near $-30$ km s$^{-1}$. The LNM forms a broad velocity bridge between two WNMs, whereas the CNM is concentrated within a narrower intermediate velocity range. CO emission is also confined to this interval and is associated with CNM-rich regions. The H I mass fractions are $\sim 41\%$, $\sim 23\%$, $\sim 17\%$, and $\sim 19\%$ for WNM1, WNM2, LNM, and CNM, respectively, with the LNM and CNM together accounting for $\sim 36\%$ of the total H I mass. No comparably independent WNM component is recovered at intermediate velocities, although a weak broad component may remain unresolved. The observed phase structures are consistent with a dynamically compressed multiphase interaction layer in which compression increases the gas density, shortens the radiative cooling time, and drives the evolution from WNM through thermally unstable gas toward the CNM. The present interaction may both create new CNM, and further compress or grow pre-existing dense structures. Our results provide the missing link between previous evidence for dynamical interaction and molecular cloud formation in Draco, and identify CNM formation as a key step in transforming gas accreting toward the Galactic disk into cold atomic and molecular clouds.

## 56. [The Connection Between the Growth of Massive Black Holes and Their Dynamical Histories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18659) <a id="link56"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18659

**Authors:** Lee A. Whitaker, Michael Tremmel, Angelo Ricarte, Priyamvada Natarajan

**Abstract:** Massive black holes (MBHs) grow through both gas accretion and mergers and it remains unclear how variations to MBH growth and dynamical histories contribute to the spread in empirical scaling relations between MBHs and their host galaxies. We examine the ex- and in-situ growth of MBHs within large-scale cosmological simulations and compare the evolution of an outlier population of under-grown MBHs to the general population. We use the Romulus25 uniform volume cosmological simulation and the RomulusC cluster zoom-in simulation to examine the growth and merger histories of MBHs. We identify central MBHs that are undergrown relative to other MBHs in similar mass galaxies and investigate their accretion, merger, and orbital histories within the simulations. The contribution of ex-situ growth increases with MBH mass, with mergers becoming increasingly important in the most massive galaxies. We find no difference between cluster and field environments in terms of the importance of ex-situ growth, which is generally very low. Under-grown MBHs have experienced fewer mergers and have had complex dynamical histories, spending significant time 'drifting' within the galaxy or 'competing' with another MBH. Other under-grown MBHs have experienced a 'late entrance' into a galaxy which until then had no central MBH. Our results demonstrate that MBH assembly is dominated by in-situ growth, which in turn is impacted by the dynamical histories of MBHs within assembling galaxies. Prolonged off-centre evolution can suppress accretion and contribute to the formation of under-grown central MBHs at $z=0$.

## 57. [Mapping Dark Matter in Merging Clusters with Weak Lensing for CSST: Morphology and Filament Recovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17857) <a id="link57"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17857

**Authors:** Xinyue Chen, Ran Li, Qiao Wang, Hengjie Lin, Liang Gao, Xiangkun Liu, Huanyuan Shan, Ji Yao, Ming Li

**Abstract:** Weak gravitational lensing offers a direct probe of the projected dark matter distribution in cluster-scale structures and the surrounding cosmic web. We investigate the capability of the China Space Station Telescope (CSST) to recover non-spherical dark matter morphologies using mock reduced-shear maps generated from cosmological N-body simulations. We apply the deep-learning-based MIU2Net mass-reconstruction framework to CSST-like wide- and deep-field source densities, focusing on two representative systems: filament-connected halo pairs and elongated halos. MIU2Net accurately recovers halo centers, elongated mass distributions, and the filamentary structures connecting halo pairs in both survey configurations. Halo centers are localized within about 0.1 h^(-1) Mpc, and elongated-halo shapes and orientations are reliably recovered. Filament paths are stable against shape noise, with mean normalized instability parameters I_F, defined as the mean deviation from the true filament locus in units of R_200, decreasing from 0.041 to 0.037 in the low-redshift bin and from 0.051 to 0.045 in the high-redshift bin from the wide to deep field. The recovered filament shapes remain consistent with the true maps at the percent level. Compared with conventional Kaiser-Squires reconstruction, MIU2Net better preserves low-contrast anisotropic structures while suppressing small-scale noise. These results demonstrate that CSST weak-lensing data, combined with machine-learning-based mass reconstruction, will enable statistical studies of cluster assembly, anisotropic matter accretion, and the connection between massive halos and the filamentary cosmic web.

## 58. [Physical characteristics of the Galaxy defining the oscillation period of librating orbits near the OLR of the bar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17255) <a id="link58"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17255

**Authors:** A. M. Melnik, E. N. Podzolkova

**Abstract:** We studied librating orbits of stars near the Outer Lindblad Resonance (OLR) of the bar using the angle-action variables of Hamiltonian dynamics. The librating orbits change the direction of their elongation relative to the major axis of the bar in a limited range of angles. To calculate orbits, we used the model of the Galaxy with an analytical bar which reproduces well the distributions of the observed stellar velocities along the Galactocentric distance R derived from the Gaia DR3 data. We compared the values of the orbital oscillation period of a typical star obtained from the theory and with the use of numerical simulation. Both values were found to be in good agreement and are equal to 2.0 Gyr. It was shown that the change in the average orbital radius, <R>, of a star, calculated over the time interval of one radial oscillation, is regulated by the change in the direction of orbital elongation. As the orbit is tilted relative to the minor axis of the bar in the sense of Galactic rotation, the angular momentum of the star, L, decreases, on average, causing a decrease in <R>. As the orbit is tilted in the opposite direction, <L> and <R> increase. We derived a general formula for the oscillation frequency, omega, of orbits captured by libration near the OLR of the bar. It turned out that the ratio of the frequency of orbit oscillations to the epicyclic frequency, omega/kappa, is determined by the parameters q_R and <p>, where the factor q_R is the ratio of the amplitude of the radial force generated by the bar to the average radial force produced by the entire galaxy at the distance of the OLR, and the parameter <p> characterizes the average elongation of orbits captured by libration near the OLR.

## 59. [Transients and Stellar Activity as Revealed by Ultraviolet Diagnostic Tracers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19313) <a id="link59"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19313

**Authors:** Richard de Grijs, Martin A. Barstow, Deepak Chahal, Fabio Favata, Xiaoting Fu, Ana I. Gómez de Castro, Sipra Hota, Firoza Sutaria

**Abstract:** Ultraviolet (UV) observations offer unique insights into transient and variable astrophysical phenomena, from the Sun and nearby stars to supernovae and star-forming galaxies. Here, we summarise and review the diagnostic power of the UV spectrum for probing the physical conditions, dynamics and energetics of these events. We trace the historical development of UV astronomy from early satellite missions to the current landscape shaped by the Hubble Space Telescope, the GALaxy Evolution eXplorer, the Neil Gehrels Swift Observatory and AstroSat/UVIT. Particular emphasis is placed on resonance lines such as Mg II $h$ and $k$, C II, Si IV, C IV and N V, which probe the (solar and) stellar chromosphere and transition region, revealing magnetic heating, shocks and mass loss across stellar types. We examine UV diagnostics of stellar flares, pre-main-sequence activity and rotation--activity correlations, linking them to exoplanetary irradiation and habitability. We then explore the UV view of supernova shock breakout and early-time emission, as well as the far-UV mapping of young stellar populations and hierarchical star formation in the Magellanic Clouds. Together, these aspects illustrate how the UV connects small-scale plasma processes to galactic-scale star formation. Despite remarkable progress, UV transient astronomy remains limited by instrument availability and cadence. Forthcoming missions promise to transform this field by capturing the rapid, luminous phenomena that define the dynamic Universe.

## 60. [SiO Maser Line-width Diagnostics of AGB Stars and Red Supergiants: The RSG-adjacent Region in the FWZI--Luminosity Plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16453) <a id="link60"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16453

**Authors:** Qiu-Yu Tao, Jun-ichi Nakashima, Yong Zhang, Zehao Zhang, Jia-Yong Xie, Chang Liu

**Abstract:** We investigate whether SiO maser line profiles can distinguish asymptotic giant branch (AGB) stars from red supergiants (RSGs) and identify unusual AGB-classified sources near the RSG regime. We compiled 2,956 detected SiO maser spectra for 1,551 objects and constructed reference samples of 1,068 O-rich AGB stars and 36 RSGs. The full width at zero intensity (FWZI) was reliably measured for 1,020 AGB stars and 32 RSGs. AGB stars are concentrated at narrow line widths: 81.5% have FWZI <10 km s^-1, whereas 81.2% of RSGs have FWZI >15 km s^-1 and none has FWZI <10 km s^-1. Infrared two-color diagrams provide only partial separation. Combining FWZI with luminosities based primarily on quality-filtered Gaia/Bailer-Jones distances, with independent literature distances for several RSGs, reveals a weak tendency for wider SiO maser lines at higher luminosities but no clean boundary. We therefore define an empirical RSG-adjacent region in the FWZI-luminosity plane, 15 <= FWZI <= 26.6 km s^-1 and 3.9 <= log(L/L_sun) <= 4.9, to characterize the high-luminosity, broad-line tail of the AGB-classified sample. Ten sources fall within this region. Using infrared SEDs, DUSTY fits, OH/H2O maser information, and variability, we find that six are most naturally interpreted as luminous AGB-like objects, two warrant follow-up to assess possible high-initial-mass AGB, RSG-like, or SAGB-related interpretations, and two are less securely constrained because of poor DUSTY fits. The RSG-adjacent region is therefore a physically mixed screening space, not a definitive classification box, for selecting high-priority follow-up targets.

## 61. [The connection between massive galaxies and their dark matter haloes from $z=0.25$ to $z \sim 4$ from the combined COSMOS, XMM-LSS and ECDF-S fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19030) <a id="link61"></a>

**Abstract:** Understanding how galaxies populate and grow within dark matter haloes is essential for linking the observed evolution of galaxies to the hierarchical growth of structure. We also find a steady increase in the satellite fraction since $z\sim 1$, from which we infer that massive halos continue to grow hierarchically while their central galaxies experience suppressed stellar-mass growth. **Abstract:** One of the unsolved questions in astrophysics and cosmology is the development of a robust physical model to explain the thermal history of the intergalactic medium during the epoch of reionization. Various astrophysical models have been proposed, but current models remain only partially constrained by available observations. To clarify this ambiguity, hydrodynamical, semi-numerical, and N-body simulations can help solve this issue. One notable simulation from the epoch of reionization is the 21SSD simulations, which provide high-resolution results for the global 21-cm brightness temperature and the 21-cm power spectrum during the epoch of reionization. The simulation includes 45 models spanning three key astrophysical parameters of X-ray emissivity, Lyman-band emissivity, and the X-ray hard-to-soft emissivity ratio. Distinct values of these parameters result in different models of this simulation. In this work, four active radio observatories, including HERA, MWA, GMRT, and LOFAR have been considered to investigate the sensitivity and thermal noise of the 21-cm power spectrum. Each observatory has a particular sensitivity based on its instrumental characteristics, which helps assess which models lie above the forecast sensitivity thresholds of current instruments.

## 65. [Turning Selection Bias into a New Statistic: Asymmetry and Fringes of Lensed Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18768) <a id="link65"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18768

**Authors:** Juno C. L. Chan, Elwin K. Y. Li

**Abstract:** Gravitational waves (GWs) propagating across the Universe can be distorted by intervening lenses, producing characteristic wave-optics signatures. These effects are not incorporated into current matched-filtering searches and can therefore substantially reduce detection efficiency. The underlying mechanism is the signal-consistency test, which measures deviations between the observed and expected signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) distributions. We report a systematic study of the impact of gravitational lensing on signal-consistency tests, using a two-image interference model and representative signal-consistency statistics in both the time and frequency domains. We find that overlapping images produce sharply localized SNR residuals in the time domain, breaking the reflection symmetry of the autocorrelation about the merger time. Although interference fringes produce broadband SNR residuals in the frequency domain, both domains exhibit a minimum for high-mass signals when the fringe structure becomes comparable to the signal bandwidth. This highlights a limitation of current strategies that search for distorted lensed signals only among GW events detected by matched-filtering searches. Exploiting this asymmetry, we develop a statistic that separates selected down-ranked lensed injections from noise-background triggers and paired nonlensed injections, without extending the template bank. Our results establish the first connection between lensing observables and detection, paving the way for the discovery of lensed GWs.

**Abstract:** The origin of Galactic cosmic rays up to the knee energy (PeV) remains a long-standing open question in astrophysics. Supernova remnant shocks are leading candidates for accelerating particles to PeV energies, yet this requires strong magnetic field amplification in the upstream region to confine the highest energy particles. Despite its importance, the upstream magnetic field strength has long lacked direct observational constraints. Here we report the observation of numerous synchrotron X-ray hotspots extending into the upstream of the supernova remnant RX J1713.7$-$3946 in archival Chandra data. These hotspots show spatial and temporal properties consistent with synchrotron emission from secondary electrons produced by hadronic interactions between accelerated protons and dense molecular clumps. Using the spatial extent of these upstream hotspots as a physical scale, we estimate the proton diffusion length to be $\sim 1~\mathrm{pc}$. This measurement provides a constraint on the upstream magnetic field strength of $\sim 10~μ\mathrm{G}$, which is consistent with recent simulations of Bell-instability-driven magnetic field amplification. Our results indicate that the current amplification is insufficient to reach PeV energies, explaining the observed TeV gamma-ray spectral cutoff and providing a diagnostic for assessing PeVatron capability in stellar shocks.

## 68. [Tidal Excitation of Relativistic Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18475) <a id="link68"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18475

**Authors:** Zhiqiang Miao, Junquan Su, Neev Khera, Huan Yang

**Abstract:** How a relativistic star responds to a time-dependent tide has lacked a first-principles counterpart of the Newtonian normal-mode expansion. In general relativity, the tidal field is gauge sensitive, radiative modes are non-Hermitian and incomplete, and the direct and late-time responses are intrinsically nonmodal. In this work we present, to our knowledge, the first causal relativistic theory that treats all of these components on equal footing. We first develop the spectral decomposition of the retarded Green function of the matched stellar interior--Schwarzschild exterior, which classifies four separate regimes in time domain: acausal region, prompt wave, surface-reflected wave, and mode+tail. The prompt signal is identical to that of a Schwarzschild black hole with the same mass, which is combined with the surface-reflected wave to be referred as the "direct part". With the Green function and a complex bilinear form on the full metric--fluid phase space, we decompose the linear response of a nonrotating perfect-fluid star into direct, quasinormal-mode, and late-time-tail sectors. Crucially, every sector admits an explicit first-order driven evolution equation: discrete bilinear projections determine the dynamically evolving quasinormal-mode amplitudes, while continuum projections of the same tidal source onto branch-cut states determine the direct and tail amplitudes. The result is a complete modal--nonmodal decomposition of the spacetime and fluid perturbations, which is physically described by the dynamics of a discrete and a continuous-frequency set of oscillators. We verify the decomposition numerically under generic time-dependent tidal driving and discuss applications to black hole perturbations.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

## 69. [Strong-Field Gravitational-Wave Lensing from Ray-Traced Arrival-Time Surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18984) <a id="link69"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18984

**Authors:** Luka Vujeva, João S. Santos, Brian C. Seymour

**Abstract:** The diffraction integral formalism has been greatly successful in describing gravitational wave lensing effects in scenarios where the wavelength of the signal is comparable to the characteristic length scale of the lens, known as the wave optics regime. However, the assumptions required to construct its integrand break down as the source approaches the strong gravity region of a black hole lens. In particular, we are interested in the lensed signal from a binary (the source) near a massive black hole (the lens). We present a new formalism for obtaining low frequency corrections to sources being lensed in the strong field regime of gravity by leveraging the fact that null geodesics obey the Hamilton--Jacobi equations. In doing so, we are able to construct a time delay surface without relying on the thin lens approximation, and solve the diffraction integral for the resulting surface directly. For a stationary source at $10M$, deep in the strong field regime, when comparing the results with a relativistic wave scattering model, we find $6.5\%$ and $0.3\%$ agreement in the peak amplification and first diffractive fringe, respectively. Furthermore, we find a $< 1\%$ agreement with the amplitude of the point mass lens with a source at $10^4 M$ in the weak field, but noticeable dephasing between the peaks of the amplification function at higher frequencies. This work allows for clean interpolation between otherwise separate regimes, valid for sources from $\sim 6M \to \infty$. It provides a transparent, physically motivated framework for computing beyond-geometric optics lensing effects in the strong field regime.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

## 70. [Identity Crisis: Untangling Mass-Ordering Artifacts from Compact-Object Pairing in Gravitational-Wave Populations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18962) <a id="link70"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18962

**Authors:** Soumendra Kishore Roy, Asad Hussain, L. A. C. van Son, Maya Fishbach, Carl L. Rodriguez, Will M. Farr

**Abstract:** Mass distributions of binary black hole mergers inferred from gravitational-wave observations offer clues to how binaries form. These distributions conventionally label the heavier component as the primary, with mass $m_1$, and the lighter as the secondary, with mass $m_2$, such that $m_1 \geq m_2$. This ordering folds the physically labeled mass distribution along the equal-mass line, potentially reshaping the underlying population structure. In this Letter, we determine which population features can arise from mass ordering and which require genuine astrophysical structure. We show that ordering alone can produce (1) an $m_2$ distribution that declines more rapidly than the $m_1$ distribution, without intrinsic differences between the physically labeled components, and (2) an apparent concentration along equal masses, without correlations that preferentially pair black holes of equal mass. Moreover, different physically labeled populations can produce the same ordered mass distribution. Crucially, however, a preferred companion mass that remains constant as $m_1$ increases requires a characteristic mass scale already present in the physically labeled population. Applied to binary black hole mergers in the fifth Gravitational-Wave Transient Catalog, our model recovers such a scale near $m_2 \simeq 50\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$. Around this scale, the merger-rate density declines much more rapidly toward higher $m_2$ than toward lower $m_2$, ruling out an equal or weaker high-$m_2$ decline at 99.45% credibility. This structure therefore requires a characteristic mass scale in at least one physically labeled component, but masses alone cannot determine which component carries it. Any formation model reproducing the observed high-mass binary black hole merger population must contain such a scale.

## 71. [Galaxy Clusters from the DESI Legacy Imaging Surveys. IV. Alignment of Member Galaxy Orientations at z < 0.5](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16265) <a id="link71"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16265

**Authors:** Zhaobin Chen, Yizhou Gu, Shufei Liu, Hu Zou, Qirong Yuan

**Abstract:** Using DESI photometric catalog data, we systematically analyze the spatial alignment of star-forming galaxies (SFGs) and quiescent galaxies (QGs) in clusters. The results reveal that, in both brightest central galaxy (BCG) alignment (BA) and satellite alignment (SA), the alignment signals of SFGs and QGs exhibit opposite trends in several aspects. For the BA signal, SFGs show stronger alignment in cluster outskirts, whereas QGs exhibit stronger alignment in inner regions. For the SA signal, both SFGs and QGs exhibit nearly isotropic signals in outer regions, while in inner regions they show opposite signals. In addition, the BA signal depends on cluster richness and QG fraction, and is more pronounced in mature clusters. Specifically, QGs show a stronger BA signal overall, which increases with luminosity and axis ratio, whereas SFGs show a weaker BA signal with no significant dependence on these parameters. The BA signal also exhibits a strong dependence on satellite galaxy color, which is independent of morphology. We confirm this by analyzing the BA signal in two populations: red disk and blue elliptical galaxies. However, neither BA nor SA shows a significant dependence on redshift at z < 0.5. In summary, these findings reflect how galaxy transport along cosmic filaments drives cluster evolution: early-stage SFGs fall into clusters along filaments, and during cluster relaxation, their spatial orientations are reshaped by gravitational potentials and tidal forces, weakening BA signal and enhancing SA signal. In contrast, QGs, as early cluster members, exhibit a more pronounced anisotropic distribution and maintain relatively stable spatial orientations.

## 72. [CosmoAgent: AI Agent for Theoretical Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16536) <a id="link72"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16536

**Authors:** Stephon Alexander, Loukas Gouskos, Savvas M. Koushiappas, Cooper Niu, Wenrong Sun

**Abstract:** We present CosmoAgent, an agentic AI framework using large language models (LLMs) to search for dark-energy and modified-gravity models. We define a covariant grammar that allows LLM agents to explore the space of modified-gravity Lagrangians. An in-house computational pipeline derives the equations of motion, solves the background dynamics, and evaluates the likelihood using late-time expansion data, including the DES 5-year type Ia supernova sample and the DESI Data Release 2 BAO measurements. Theories are ranked by the Akaike Information Criterion (AIC) to balance simplicity and likelihood. We benchmarked 8 frontier LLMs from ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, Kimi, DeepSeek, and Qwen, and all LLMs are able to find theories with a lower nominal AIC than flat $Λ$CDM, with a best improvement of $Δ$ AIC=-3.328. Seven recover quadratic quintessence. Exploratory searches also return induced-gravity and scalar-Gauss-Bonnet structures. The modest AIC improvements do not establish an observational preference for these models over flat $Λ$CDM. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/wsunamoutlook-prog/AI-Modified-Gravity

## 73. [Nonlinear Perturbations of Relativistic Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18952) <a id="link73"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18952

**Authors:** K. J. Kwon, Abhishek Hegade K. R., Tejaswi Venumadhav, Hang Yu, Nicolás Yunes

**Abstract:** We extend the general relativistic Lagrangian perturbation formalism of Friedman and Schutz [Astrophys. J. 200, 204 (1975)] beyond linear order, retaining full time dependence of the perturbations. We provide general formulae to relate the Lagrangian and Eulerian perturbations to any general perturbative order. As a proof of principle, we apply the framework to tidally perturbed relativistic stars in Regge-Wheeler gauge and reduce the second-order field and conservation equations to master equations with a nonlinear source. Projecting the source onto the star's eigenmodes, we obtain a nonlinear equation of motion for the mode amplitudes and the coupling coefficients, which describe the interactions among the eigenmodes and with the external tidal field. Approximating the tidal field and the coupling coefficients, we compute the static quadratic Love number from the quadrupolar fundamental mode alone and find that it reproduces the direct metric-matching value to a reasonable accuracy of $4\%$, up to a further correction arising from the Hamiltonian and momentum constraints of the second-order tidal field.

## 74. [Grand minima and intermittency in near-critical convection-driven dynamos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16314) <a id="link74"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16314

**Authors:** P. J. Käpylä

**Abstract:** Context: Solar magnetism exhibits long-term variations on timescales of decades to centuries. Aims: The main aim is to study how the supercriticality of the dynamo affects the variability of magnetic fields on timescales relevant to the Sun. Methods: Three-dimensional rotating magnetohydrodynamic simulations in a spherical wedge geometry are used to produce self-consistent convection-driven dynamos. The supercriticality of the dynamo is controlled by varying the magnetic diffusivity. Results: The main result is that long-term variability of the dynamo increases with decreasing supercriticality of the dynamo and that cases nearest to the onset of dynamo action show extended grand minima-like periods of low activity. The simulations fall in three classes where the least supercritical cases show the largest variations of magnetic activity and where the flow including differential rotation is almost unaffected by the magnetic field. In the cases with the highest supercriticality the magnetic field is permanently strong and differential rotation permanently quenched, both having relatively little temporal variation. In an intermediate regime both the magnetic fields and differential rotation exhibit strong anticorrelated fluctuations. Conclusions: The occurrence of grand minima in the Sun has been conjectured to be due to a weakly supercritical dynamo. The current results support this idea and suggest that the absence of grand minima in earlier global dynamo simulations is because their large-scale dynamos were too supercritical. Furthermore, the current results suggest that the effect of the magnetic field on differential rotation is negligible if the average rms-field is less than about 2~kG.

## 75. [Black Hole Fine Structure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18989) <a id="link75"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18989

**Authors:** Adrian Ka-Wai Chung, Richard Dyer, Tomasz Kinowski, Christopher J. Moore

**Abstract:** In General Relativity, axial and polar black-hole perturbations share identical quasinormal-mode spectra, a property known as isospectrality. New physics can break this degeneracy, producing a spectral splitting analogous to atomic fine structure. We perform a theory-agnostic search for such splitting in gravitational-wave observations. We find no evidence for isospectrality breaking and place a combined $90\%$ upper limit of $<28.5\%$ on the relative frequency splitting. We map these constraints onto several modified-gravity theories, constraining the coupling-lengths to be $\lesssim 28\,\mathrm{km}$.

## 76. [Scaling Up: A Global Optical/X-ray Correlation For Reprocessing Across Black Hole Masses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17248) <a id="link76"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17248

**Authors:** Changhyun Cho, David M. Russell, Aaryan Sharma, Payaswini Saikia, Maria Cristina Baglio, Kevin Alabarta, Sandeep K. Rout, Yi-Jung Yang, Eleonora Caruso, Mokhine Motsoaledi, Meriana Allah, Ian M. McHardy

**Abstract:** This study extends the optical/X-ray correlation from black hole low-mass X-ray binaries (XRBs) to active galactic nuclei (AGN), bridging scales from stellar-mass to supermassive black holes (SMBHs). In XRBs, optical emission predominantly originates from the thermal reprocessing of high-energy photons on the surface of the geometrically thin, optically thick accretion disk. The reprocessed optical luminosity follows a power-law relation of the form $L_{V} \propto L_{X}^β a$, with $β\sim 0.5$, where $L_X$ is the X-ray luminosity and the orbital separation $a$ serves as a proxy for the disk size. To test the universality of this relationship across all mass scales, we analyzed quasi-simultaneous X-ray and optical flux data from a sample of XRBs and AGN that have estimates of the radius of the optically emitting disk. To bridge the structural differences between these populations, we utilized the characteristic optical/X-ray time lag derived from reverberation mapping as a physical proxy for the reprocessing radius. Our analysis reveals a tight, global correlation across all black hole masses, described by $\log L_V = 0.48\log L_X + 0.96\log a_R + 6.5$ with a scatter of $\sim 0.20$ dex, where $a_R$ is the reprocessing radius derived from the lag. Incorporating a geometric correction for the disk inclination angle ($\cos i$) slightly reduces this scatter to $0.18$ dex. These results suggest that X-ray reprocessing is scale-invariant across accretion disks, from stellar-mass black holes to SMBHs. This scaling relation provides new constraints for models of X-ray emission regions in accreting black holes.

## 77. [Cosmological Consequences of a Highly Decoupled Right-handed Neutrino](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18334) <a id="link77"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18334

**Authors:** Stephen F. King, Rishav Roshan, Sudipta Show

**Abstract:** In the minimal type-I seesaw, one right-handed neutrino (RHN) can decouple from neutrino mass generation. Such a state is usually neglected, yet its feeble couplings make it long-lived, and it can dominate the energy density of the Universe before decaying. We study this in a minimal $U(1)_{B-L}$ model, where the RHNs are produced through $B-L$ gauge interactions, for the cases of a decoupled lightest RHN $N_1$ and a decoupled heaviest RHN $N_3$. The resulting early matter-dominated era dilutes the baryon asymmetry, and for decoupled $N_3$ it also delays the equilibration of the charged-lepton Yukawa interactions, allowing a lighter $N_1$ to generate the asymmetry in the unflavoured regime. Breaking $U(1)_{B-L}$ forms cosmic strings whose gravitational-wave background acquires a spectral break from the early matter-dominated era, with a frequency set by the decoupled RHN mass and the lightest neutrino mass, within reach of future detectors such as SKA, THEIA, LISA, $μ$ARES, DECIGO and ET.

## 78. [TractorJAX: GPU-Accelerated SPHEREx Forced Photometry and the Trade-offs of Regularization and External Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16007) <a id="link78"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16007

**Authors:** Hyeonguk Bahk, Ho Seong Hwang

**Abstract:** We present TractorJAX, an open-source GPU reimplementation of the catalog-forced mode of The Tractor. We use it to test whether regularization or external flux priors enable reliable fitting of reference catalogs that extend below the depth directly constrained by undersampled SPHEREx images. For full-depth regularized fits, TractorJAX fits $100\times100$-pixel cutouts at 11.9 ms each on a single NVIDIA H100 GPU. In simulated images of the Abell 2537 galaxy-cluster field, extending the fitted catalog from $m_z<21$ to the full DESI Legacy Imaging Surveys DR10 catalog increases the scatter in fractional flux residuals by 34%, even with regularization. We also test the methods using SPHEREx observations in nine fields, assessing the number of reliably measured sources and photometric-redshift accuracy. Requiring S/N $>5$ in more than half of the 102 channels, we find that the regularized full-depth fit yields 40% fewer sources than the truncated fit. For sources meeting this requirement in both fits, the normalized median absolute deviation of the photometric redshifts, $σ_{\rm NMAD}$, is 17% larger in the full-depth fit. External flux priors recover nearly as many sources as the $m_z<21$ fit, but increase $σ_{\rm NMAD}$ by 0.0015 for the common calibrated sample. Neither regularization nor the external flux priors tested here simultaneously preserve the sample size and photometric-redshift accuracy achieved by the truncated fit. Our results suggest that the optimal fitted-catalog depth cannot be determined from image-level performance alone, but should be selected through end-to-end tests tailored to the intended science measurement.

## 79. [$\texttt{NFDiag}$: High-dimensional MCMC Convergence Diagnosis with Normalizing Flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16574) <a id="link79"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16574

**Authors:** Kutay Nazlı, Elena Sellentin

**Abstract:** Accurately assessing the convergence of Monte Carlo Markov Chains is crucial for ensuring their validity in Bayesian inference problems. However, convergence assessment in high dimensions is known to be difficult, due to most convergence diagnostics becoming insensitive beyond a few dozen parameters. Conventional convergence statistics analyze marginal distributions which in high dimensions neglects increasing levels of information from the joint and conditional probability distributions. Thus we propose $\texttt{NFDiag}$, which taps into the information of the full multivariate joint posterior with a normalizing flow (NF). Our convergence diagnostic thereby also analyzes information from conditional distributions. In other words, by working in the full multivariate space, it is sensitive with respect to the geometry of the chain in high dimensions. Our diagnostic utilizes that NFs assume independent and identically distributed (iid) training samples, which is a testable assumption that will be violated by a non-converged or strongly correlated chain. Our diagnostic trains NFs on separate parts of a chain, and then assesses whether these correspond to the same multivariate target distribution. We test our diagnostic in up to 250 dimensions, and demonstrate it is universal and unbiased, and detects problematic chain quality also when Gelman-Rubin $\hat{R}$ and the multivarite effective sample size do not.

**Abstract:** Binaries composed of a luminous OB star and a black hole (BH) are a key evolutionary link between massive stellar binaries and merging compact-object binaries, but only a handful are known and their occurrence fraction remains poorly constrained. We use multi-epoch spectroscopy from the SDSS-V Milky Way Mapper to place a first rigorous empirical upper limit on the fraction of Galactic massive ($\geq 10\,\mathrm{M}_\odot$) OB stars with a close BH companion, $f_\mathrm{BH}$. Radial-velocity (RV) variability alone cannot distinguish BH companions from the far more numerous luminous binaries. We therefore combine the SDSS-V RV variability measurements of 3130 massive OB stars with high-resolution follow-up and literature information to identify luminous companions among the RV-variable subset. Forward-modelling the detectability of OB+BH systems using BH masses and orbital parameters from binary-evolution models, we find $f_\mathrm{BH} \lesssim 3\%$ at 95% credibility. This empirical upper limit approaches, but does not exclude, theoretical predictions for the OB+BH fraction. The constraint is primarily limited by the number of RV-variable systems that can be decisively classified, highlighting the importance of complete, high-quality follow-up for future searches.

## 81. [Are Broad Mg II Galaxies Fading AGN?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19126) <a id="link81"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19126

**Authors:** Stephanie M. LaMassa, Michael Eracleous, Jessie Runnoe, Benny Trakhtenbrot, Gisella de Rosa

**Abstract:** We analyzed a sample of 494 galaxies from the Sloan Digital Sky Survey (SDSS) I-IV that have unusual properties. They display broadened Mg II $λ$2798 line emission, as would be expected of Type 1 active galactic nuclei (AGN). However, they have weak or absent broad Balmer lines though broadened Balmer lines often accompany broadened ultraviolet lines in Type 1 AGN spectra. We performed a spectral decomposition of these "broad Mg II galaxies'' to assess whether they can be considered fading AGN. The Balmer lines are more responsive to changes in the AGN continuum than the Mg II line. As the AGN power source fades, broad H$β$ and then H$α$ will weaken and eventually disappear, leaving broad Mg II as a signpost of a more active past. In support of this interpretation, we found that broad Mg II galaxies have weaker AGN luminosities and lower accretion rates (parameterized by the Eddington ratio) than typical Type 1 quasars from SDSS. The Eddington ratio of broad Mg II galaxies are similar to dim-state changing-look AGN, with an average value $\lesssim$1\%, which is the threshold at which the inner region of a geometrically thin accretion disk is thought to evaporate and be replaced by a geometrically thick radiatively inefficient accretion flow in stellar-mass black holes. Broad Mg II galaxies may represent the penultimate stage in a changing-look AGN sequence prior to the complete disappearance of all broad lines.

## 82. [The WDM time-frequency transform in gravitational-wave data analysis II: Evolutionary spectrum and noise covariance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19002) <a id="link82"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19002

**Authors:** Aaron D. Johnson, Katerina Chatziioannou, Robert Rosati

**Abstract:** Noise cannot be treated as stationary over the timescales required for upcoming gravitational-wave detectors, minutes for ground-based and months for space-based detectors. Nonstationarity leads to a non-diagonal frequency-domain noise covariance matrix, thus motivating a shift to the time-frequency domain. We introduce \emph{evolutionary power spectra} for describing nonstationary noise in the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer time-frequency basis. Under the framework of oscillatory processes, we show that, to leading order in the ratio of the Meyer filter and the nonstationarity timescales, the evolutionary spectrum is a power-frequency distribution that can be estimated from a single noise realization. We derive the corresponding noise covariance matrix and show that it is band tri-diagonal, like its stationary counterpart. The exact covariance to all orders is positive semi-definite by construction and dense depending on the noise. We further derive the optimal tiling of the time-frequency plane that minimizes the error of a diagonal covariance approximation and show that coarse-graining the plane suppresses correlations quadratically. Using simulated data with a LISA Galactic foreground and a drifting powerlaw spectrum, we show that the stationarity approximation leads to signal-to-noise ratio loss and severely biases inference. For realistic LISA data, approximating the covariance as diagonal in the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer domain is accurate for the narrow-band analysis of a single resolved Galactic binary, but insufficient for a broad-band analysis. Finally, we provide mitigation strategies for the wraparound correlations induced across the data time edges whenever the spectrum differs between the start and the end of the data.

**Abstract:** Tidal Love numbers characterize the conservative response of compact objects to external perturbations and provide a powerful probe of their nature. Exploiting a recent breakthrough in the separability of these perturbations, we analytically determine the static tidal response of the most general stationary black hole in four-dimensional Einstein--Maxwell theory. For arbitrary multipole order, charge, and spin, we show that the entire matrix of conservative Love numbers vanishes, including the mixed gravito-electromagnetic couplings. ## 84. [The Forgetful Black Hole: Memory Burden and Hysteresis in Hawking Evaporation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18997) <a id="link84"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18997

**Authors:** Yen Chin Ong

**Abstract:** The memory-burden scenario of black hole evaporation proposes that Hawking emission may become increasingly suppressed as maintaining stored quantum information becomes dynamically costly. In this work, we revisit this scenario and investigate the conditions under which information retention can produce a genuine dynamical burden. We derive general scaling conditions for incremental memory accumulation at a fixed fractional mass loss and establish a conditional no-go result: entropy-suppressed, zero-mean deposits with subextensive integrated correlations cannot produce an order-one luminosity modification through unamplified accumulation and bounded feedback. We identify three essential requirements for dynamical memory: a record of the past must be written, retained, and coupled back to subsequent evaporation. We illustrate these requirements with concrete toy models that exhibit either a prolonged but finite black hole lifetime or an asymptotically approached finite-mass state (an effective remnant). We further distinguish kinetic-memory, thermodynamic-memory, and spectral-memory sub-categories of possible memory-burden mechanisms. In particular, we demonstrate that an independently evolving memory variable can generate evaporation-accretion hysteresis, such that a black hole returning to the same mass may possess a different retained memory and consequently a different luminosity. This history dependence may have implications for primordial black hole phenomenology. Finally, we briefly discuss the possibility of "merger rejuvenation", in which black hole mergers reorganize or reset the effective memory state.

## 85. [Enabling Polyspectra via Stochastic Realizations of Inverse Decay Curvature Perturbations in Axion Inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18748) <a id="link85"></a>

**Abstract:** Current and upcoming galaxy-redshift surveys offer a promising path for constraining inflationary physics. These constraints are often imposed by relating correlations of the primordial curvature perturbation to the late-time galaxy correlation functions. The primordial correlators can either be computed analytically, or via realizations of the primordial curvature perturbations that are initialized on a grid and evolved numerically according to the classical equations of motion. In this work we take a hybrid approach, initializing the fields in the Bunch-Davies vacuum; and then imposing time evolution through direct numerical integration. Our method efficiently generates realizations of the inverse decay primordial curvature perturbations for axion inflation---an inflationary model in which a pseudoscalar inflaton field couples to a $U(1)$ gauge field via a Chern-Simons interaction. Depending on the choice of parameter $ξ\in\{2, 2.5, 3\}$, which controls gauge field particle production in this model, our method generates realizations with an average runtime of a few hours on 1 CPU core. We compute the power spectrum and bispectrum from these realizations and find a surprisingly large discrepancy between our result and previous work that used an approximation for the gauge field mode functions---up to a factor of $\sim4$ difference for the bispectrum in the case of $ξ=2$. This new method enables not only rapid computation of higher order polyspectra, but also generation of initial conditions for large-scale structure analyses.

**Abstract:** While the spin vectors of binary black holes offer insight into the astrophysical formation channels of these systems, individual spins are less precisely measured through gravitational-wave observations than the effective inspiral spin $χ_\mathrm{eff}$ and the effective precession spin $χ_\mathrm{p}$. Here we show how to quantify whether individual mergers carry information about component spins beyond the constraints implied by effective parameters (a.k.a. spinformation) and apply the technique to GWTC-5. Only $\sim3\%$ of gravitational-wave events have substantial spin information not explained by $χ_\mathrm{eff}$ and $χ_\mathrm{p}$. The catalog's component spin information is effectively two-dimensional in $χ_\mathrm{eff}$ and $χ_\mathrm{p}$. Using simulated signals, we find the noise realization can create or destroy over a bit of beyond-$χ_\mathrm{eff}$ information: the structure in real data may therefore be driven by noise. At current sensitivity, component spins provide interpretive\textemdash rather than informational\textemdash advantage over effective parameters.

## 87. [Fast Radio Bursts from Monster Shocks and Flares of Inspiraling Binary Neutron Stars: A Kinetic Model for Double Pulsar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18208) <a id="link87"></a>

**Authors:** Jan Benáček, Benoît Cerutti, Martin Pohl, Tim Dietrich, Anna Neuweiler

**Abstract:** The coalescence of binary neutron star systems offers unprecedented possibilities for multimessenger observations, including the detection of gravitational waves, gamma-ray bursts, kilonovae, and potentially fast radio bursts. We present a three-dimensional particle-in-cell kinetic simulation of the combined magnetosphere of a binary neutron star in which the two stars physically orbit one another so that the orbital motion itself twists the magnetic loops. We find that the stellar magnetic-field intensity ratio controls the emergence of flares, while the stellar spins set the direction and modulation of the flare. We identify four spatially and dynamically distinct regions of strong electromagnetic energy release: (i) an expanding fast magnetosonic wave that steepens into a monster shock, (ii) a plasma flare being pushed by (iii) an expanding fireball and containing (iv) a reconnecting current sheet. The fast magnetosonic wave is a promising source of coherent radio emission. It is released when orbital motion cuts a closed plasma loop. The electric and magnetic wave amplitudes are similar and reach that of the background magnetic field. Our kinetic treatment yields particle spectra and plasma structures that can be used to assess coherent emission in kinetic-scale simulations. In conclusion, the stellar orbital motion constitutes a driving channel distinct from models with spin-driven magnetospheric twisting.

## 88. [Universal relations for hybrid stars and their quark cores: a systematic scan of the quark-matter parameter space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17230) <a id="link88"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17230

**Authors:** J. P. Carlomagno, G. A. Contrera, A. G. Grunfeld, G. Lugones

**Abstract:** Universal, equation-of-state (EOS) insensitive relations among the global properties of compact stars are well established for hadronic neutron stars and for self-bound strange stars. We ask whether analogous relations hold for the quark core of a hybrid star, and whether the core can be constrained from the surface. Describing quark matter with the three-flavor MIT bag model with repulsive vector interactions, matched to the hadronic DD2 EOS through a Maxwell construction, we scan the full $(B^{1/4},G_V)$ plane and obtain 1112 hybrid EOSs with maximum masses above $2\,M_\odot$, broadly compatible with current multimessenger constraints. For every stable configuration we evaluate the mass, radius, moment of inertia, and tidal deformability both at the stellar surface and at the boundary of the quark core. Global quantities follow the relations of hadronic stars---the $I$--Love relation to within $0.5\%$---while core quantities follow those of self-bound strange stars with median deviations of $6$--$9\%$. We explain the latter by expanding the structure of the core in powers of its compactness: to first order, the core mass, baryon mass, moment of inertia, and tidal deformability depend only on the compactness and on the properties of the hadron--quark interface, chiefly the ratio $ξ=p_{\rm tr}/\varepsilon_+$ of transition pressure to quark energy density. The strange-star relations are the $ξ\to0$ member of this family, and the deviations from them measure $ξ$. The fractional core mass and radius of all configurations follow the normalized mass profile of the star, and no global observable allows the core size to be inferred. Evanescent cores dominate the viable parameter space, while sizable cores require low bag constants and strong vector couplings.

## 89. [Evidence for Distinct One-Off and Repeating Subpopulations in CHIME/FRB Catalog 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18710) <a id="link89"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18710

**Authors:** Sloane Sirota, Duncan R. Lorimer

**Abstract:** The Canadian Hydrogen Intensity Mapping Experiment (CHIME) has published its second catalog of 3,641 fast radio bursts (FRBs). While this sample includes 981 bursts from 83 repeating sources, only 2.3% of CHIME FRBs are observed to repeat. This raises the question of whether the FRB population truly consists of a single repeating population or includes a distinct subpopulation of one-off sources. We apply Simulation Based Inference (SBI), a likelihood-free machine learning framework, to model the CHIME/FRB Catalog 2 dataset, testing eight FRB population models, half of which include a subpopulation of truly one-off sources. The current catalog strongly and decisively favors models including such a subpopulation, with the best-fitting model combining a log-normal burst rate distribution, a Poissonian wait-time distribution, and a one-off subpopulation. This preference is corroborated by an independent random forest classifier and by the ability of one-off models to naturally reproduce the hyperactive repeating sources present in the catalog without having been trained on them. We place upper limits on the potentially observable CHIME/FRB population of 36,000 repeating sources and 19 one-off sources per day in the CHIME sky. Models with a one-off subpopulation naturally account for the hyperactive repeating sources present in the catalog without having been trained on them, providing an independent consistency check on our results. We predict the repeater percentage will fall below 2% in the next five years, eventually declining to ~0.5% as the sample saturates with one-off detections.

## 90. [Cosmic Colliders in Binary Blazars Probing Ultra-High-Energy Particle Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17033) <a id="link90"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17033

**Authors:** Giorgio Galanti

**Abstract:** While terrestrial colliders and fixed-target cosmic-ray interactions are bounded at center-of-mass (CM) energies $\sqrt{s} \lesssim \mathcal{O}(10^5)\,\text{GeV}$, we demonstrate that bound sub-parsec binary blazars form broad-band, beam-beam hadronic ``Cosmic Colliders'', overcoming such limitation. Driven by modest jet misalignments ($θ_{\rm mis} \sim 1^\circ - 6^\circ$), co-streaming proton beams collide at CM energies reaching $\sqrt{s} \sim 10^{10}\,\text{GeV}$ - six orders of magnitude above the Large Hadron Collider. Operating in a strictly optically thin regime ($τ_{pp} \ll 1$), the system performs a continuous CM energy scan, probing asymptotic QCD scaling, gluon saturation, and Froissart unitarity, while opening a possible high-sensitivity window into physics beyond the Standard Model. Secondary PeV-EeV neutrinos and sub-TeV $γ$-rays fall directly within reach of current and planned observatories such as LHAASO, CTAO, IceCube, and GRAND, establishing binary blazars as laboratories for physics at the ultimate energy limit.

## 91. [Radio and Gamma-Ray Timing of a Newly Discovered Millisecond Pulsar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17247) <a id="link91"></a>

**Authors:** L. Vleeschower, R. Sengar, L. Nieder, C. J. Clark, D. L. Kaplan, A. Anumarlapudi, R. Girgaonkar, T. Thongmeearkom, G. Y. Agazie, M. Bailes, E. D. Barr, R. P. Breton, M. E. Decesar, T. Dolch, M. Kramer, R. S. Lynch, E. Parent, S. M. Ransom, B. W. Stappers, J. van Leeuwen

**Abstract:** We report the discovery and timing of PSR J2147-3604, a fully recycled millisecond pulsar with a spin period of 3.2 ms, discovered in a reprocessing of the 350-MHz Green Bank North Celestial Cap (GBNCC) survey and later serendipitously confirmed by the MeerKAT TRAPUM UHF survey of Fermi-LAT unidentified sources. We followed up this pulsar with Murriyang/Parkes radio telescope and obtained a phase-connected timing solution spanning one year, revealing a 4.5-day binary orbit with very low eccentricity (e ~ 1.9 x $10^{-5}$). A follow-up search in LAT data revealed gamma-ray pulsations, allowing for a joint radio and gamma-ray timing analysis. Assuming a neutron-star mass of 1.5 Msun, the inferred median companion mass (~0.2 Msun) is consistent with a helium white dwarf. The pulsar has a low dispersion measure of 17.0 pc cm$^{-3}$ and shows strong scintillation, leading to it being detected in ~72% of the observations covering the 704-1174 MHz band with Murriyang. The pulsar has a narrow duty cycle of ~6.5%, which leads to individual time of arrival (ToA) uncertainties of ~4.5 us for a 10 min integration with MeerKAT, suggesting this pulsar is well suited for inclusion in PTA experiments.

## 92. [The Promise of Gravitational-Wave Astronomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17647) <a id="link92"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17647

**Authors:** Parameswaran Ajith

**Abstract:** We review the promise of upcoming gravitational-wave (GW) observations in addressing some of the important open questions in astrophysics and cosmology. A decade after the first direct detection of GWs, the field is entering a transformative phase driven by progressive detector upgrades and the anticipated advent of space-based and next-generation ground-based observatories. These instruments will not only push the distance horizon of GW observations to cosmological redshifts, but will dramatically extend the accessible frequency band, opening entirely new source classes that lie beyond the reach of current detectors. The source landscape is rich enough that surprises are likely. This review surveys our current understanding across this landscape and offers some perspective on the discoveries that upcoming GW observations are poised to deliver.

## 93. [Dynamical formation of dormant black holes: Predictions for Gaia DR4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18601) <a id="link93"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18601

**Authors:** Daniel Marín Pina, Michela Mapelli, Giuliano Iorio, Cecilia Sgalletta

**Abstract:** Dormant black holes (dormant BHs, i.e., X-ray quiet binaries comprising a BH and a companion star) are probes of the effect of cluster dynamics and binary stellar evolution on compact-object binaries. A dozen dormant BH binaries are known, a number that will increase substantially with the upcoming Gaia Data Release 4 (DR4). Their origin is uncertain, and dynamical formation is proposed as one possible channel. Here, we study the formation of dormant BHs in low-mass star clusters, accounting for the interplay of binary stellar evolution and cluster dynamics. We present new simulations with the $N$-body code PeTar, interfaced with the population synthesis code SEVN for single and binary star evolution. Our grid spans a range of cluster masses ($300-4800\,$M$_\odot$), metallicities ($1.4\times10^{-2}$, $1.4\times10^{-4}$), and natal kick models (with and without fallback). We find that almost all dormant BH binaries escape the parent cluster within $\sim1\,$Gyr ($\sim10\,$Myr) when assuming weak (strong) natal kicks. Most ($73\%$) dormant BHs form via the exchange of a BH (or its progenitor star) into a stellar binary, or of a star into a binary with a BH. Dormant BHs in low-mass clusters have a wide orbital period distribution (from a few hours to $10^8\,$d), with an upper limit set by natal kicks, a bimodal eccentricity distribution (a first peak at zero eccentricity for circularised systems and a second at high eccentricity), and stellar masses that follow the (evolved) initial mass function. Large BH masses are a smoking gun of dynamical formation, via exchanges and stellar collisions. We expect Gaia DR4 to contain $\sim1-64$ high-metallicity and $\sim0.4-48$ low-metallicity dormant BHs, with a period distribution peaking at $\sim10^3\,$d, $\gtrsim90\%$ of them with eccentricity $e>0.1$, and $\sim20\%$ with large BH masses ($m_{\rm BH} \gtrsim 30\,$M$_\odot$).
