## 94. [Dynamically Coupled Three-dimensional Cloud-Chemistry in Rapidly Rotating Irradiated Atmospheres: an Application to the Compact White Dwarf-Brown Dwarf system ZTFJ0038+2030](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17714) <a id="link94"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17714

**Authors:** Yanhong Lai, Xianyu Tan, Daphne Broski-Laing, Yifan Zhou, Sarah L. Casewell, Jenni R. French, Elspeth K. H. Lee, Joshua D. Lothringer, Vivien Parmentier, Shang-Min Tsai

**Abstract:** Clouds and disequilibrium chemistry shape the observable properties of substellar and planetary atmospheres, and JWST time-resolved spectroscopy now enables direct tests of their three-dimensional structures. We present an update to the 3D atmospheric framework ADAM, coupling the dynamical core to a miniature kinetic chemistry scheme (mini-chem) and a saturation-relaxation tracer cloud module. Using this framework, we investigate the atmosphere of the brown dwarf orbiting the white dwarf ZTFJ0038+2030 in a 10-hour orbit. The brown dwarf receives stellar irradiation comparable to its internal heat flux, placing the atmosphere in a regime where the circulation is characterized by a standing Matsuno--Gill wave response and equatorial superrotation. Rapid rotation confines these structures closer to the equator, producing more symmetric broadband light curves with smaller hotspot offsets than canonical hot Jupiters, in agreement with recent JWST observations of this system. Although most species are globally homogenized, rapid rotation limits global-scale horizontal mixing and allows circulation-driven vertical transport to maintain strong CO and CO$_2$ inhomogeneities in the photosphere. These chemical inhomogeneities produce asymmetric phase curves in the CO and CO$_2$ absorption bands, which could qualitatively explain the observed CO$_2$ light-curve asymmetry. When clouds are included, Na$_2$S clouds dominate the photosphere and weaken the large-scale circulation, lowering photospheric CO and CO$_2$ abundances, while MgSiO$_3$ and Fe clouds rain out at deeper levels. Because the dominant Na$_2$S clouds reside mostly above the quench region, cloud-driven convection has limited impact on the quenched abundances, which are instead regulated mainly by large-scale circulation.

## 95. [jax-SNANA: A GPU-accelerated Supernova Framework for the SBI Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19000) <a id="link95"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19000

**Authors:** Matthew Grayling

**Abstract:** We present jax-SNANA, a JAX reimplementation of the supernova analysis framework SNANA. jax-SNANA enables GPU-accelerated transient simulations and light curve fitting, supernova standardisation and cosmological inference. Running in single precision on an NVIDIA A100 GPU, jax-SNANA simulates more than 100,000 LSST-observed supernovae per second and fits 5,000 light curves per second after JIT compilation, a two orders-of-magnitude increase over current tools, essential for analysing samples at the scale expected from LSST. jax-SNANA currently supports the SALT and BayeSN spectral energy distribution (SED) models as well as physical models from redback-jax and a variety of SN SED templates; light curve fitting is supported with both photometric and spectroscopic redshifts. We fully validate the accuracy of jax-SNANA by comparing to SNANA, finding excellent consistency between the two across a variety of tasks. The code provides a modular JAX end-to-end supernova cosmology pipeline, user-extensible to support new features including different SED templates and photometric classification algorithms. jax-SNANA has been designed with future applications of simulation-based inference in mind, providing fast simulations and enabling fully compiled on-the-fly simulations to be generated within the training loop of downstream SBI tasks. jax-SNANA extends beyond SNANA by supporting simulations of generic SN-host galaxy relationships and multiple sub-populations of events, for example originating from young and old progenitors. jax-SNANA is publicly available at https://github.com/mattgrayling/jax-snana and installable through pip.

## 96. [Kinetic Theory of the EFTofLSS : A Halo-Based Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19001) <a id="link96"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19001

**Authors:** Om Kishor Hebbar, Caio B. de S. Nascimento, Arka Banerjee

**Abstract:** The Effective Field Theory of Large Scale Structure (EFTofLSS) provides a coarse-grained description of the non-linear gravitational clustering of dark matter, in which the effects of short-scale physics are encoded in an effective stress tensor and a set of counterterms. In this work, we develop a framework to investigate the physical origin of these counterterms by relating the effective stress tensor to the dynamics of individual dark matter halos in cosmological N-body simulations. We find that halo accretion contributes to the trace of the halo-based effective stress tensor, contributing to the background pressure and effective sound speed, while anisotropic accretion generates the traceless part, consequently contributing to the shear viscosity. For the halo population with $M_\mathrm{vir}>10^{12}\,M_\odot/h$, we find that halos account for $\sim\!95\%$, $\sim\!85\%$, and $\sim\!70\%$ of the full box background pressure, effective sound speed, and shear viscosity, respectively, depending on the EFT cutoff scale. The contributions to the counterterms are dominated by cluster-scale halos, $M_\mathrm{vir} \sim 10^{14} - 10^{15}\,M_\odot/h$ and halos with larger inflow rates, supporting an interpretation in terms of accretion-driven, out-of-equilibrium dynamics. Our results provide a short-scale, kinetic-theory interpretation of the EFTofLSS counterterms and establish a framework for connecting the properties and dynamics of short-scale structures to physics-informed priors for large-scale cosmological observables.

## 97. [A breath of fresh air: Oxygen enhancement from silicate sublimation during early protostellar evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17921) <a id="link97"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17921

**Authors:** F. J. Ballieux, M. K. McClure, Ł. Tychoniec, L. Francis, A. Egliene, E. A. Bergin, M. R. Van 't Hoff, E. F. Van Dishoeck

**Abstract:** Recently, an analog to the t = 0 moment of our Solar System's meteoritic record was identified for the first time around the Class I protostar HOPS-315. Crystalline silicates were found to recondense from SiO gas that cools as it advects upward from a hot thermostat region in the inner midplane. This t=0 moment may be associated with the formation of the first planetesimals in this disk. We explore whether HOPS-68, a Class I protostar with similar properties and system geometries, is engaged in such refractory recondensation as well. A detection of cooler gas-phase SiO in absorption above a hot disk midplane would allow us to achieve this goal, thanks to a previous detection of crystalline silicates. HOPS-68 was observed with the JWST MIRI and NIRSpec, showing multiple bands of molecular absorption. We use local thermal equilibrium slab models to measure the temperature, column density, velocity, and radial location of these bands. The molecular absorption bands of gaseous SiO, H2O, CO2, and 13CO trace a warm molecular inversion layer (300-500 K) at mid-altitudes above the hot disk midplane from 0.3-16 AU, which is consistently blue-shifted with respect to the system velocity by 33-53 km/s. Atacama Large Millimeter/submillimeter Array (ALMA) observations of HOPS-68 kinematically resolve an SiO-rich jet and wind. We confirm that HOPS-68, like HOPS-315, is undergoing an epoch analogous to the t=0 moment in the Solar System's planet formation timeline. The detected warm molecular layer traces the base of a disk wind, which carries sublimated material from the thermostat region near 2 AU up into the outer disk. As for HOPS-315, the molecular layer in the inner few AU of HOPS-68 is enhanced in bulk oxygen as a result of this sublimation. Since HOPS-68 is less evolved than HOPS-315, this may indicate a prolonged epoch in protostellar evolution in which such oxygen enhancement occurs.

**Abstract:** Aims. We have mapped the dust and molecular gas emission distributions in the vicinity (a few stellar radii) of the asymptotic giant branch (AGB) star EP\,Aquarii, with a previously reported stellar companion at $\sim$50\,au, to search for binary-induced asymmetries. Methods. We present an analysis of new high-angular-resolution ($\gtrsim$4\,mas) images obtained with MATISSE (in the wavelength range $\sim$3--8\,\mum) as well as archival high-angular-resolution ($\gtrsim$20\,mas) ALMA $\rm^{12}$CO\,(c$J$=2-1) line and continuum emission observations ($\sim$230\,GHz), to study the circumstellar morphology and kinematics of the molecular gas on these spatial scales. Results. The MATISSE images display a bright central source and two slightly extended emission features ("blobs") located on opposite sides of the AGB star (at radial distances of $\sim$4--10\,au). These structures are consistent with previous mid-infrared interferometric observations, suggesting stability over decades. The central source is elongated along the direction defined by the blobs, in both the MATISSE and ALMA continuum images. The ALMA $\rm^{12}$CO\,($J$=2-1) maps display an asymmetric inner wind with a velocity gradient. Conclusions. The observations are consistent with an edge-on dusty equatorial overdensity (disc-like), hinting the presence of a close ($\lesssim$4\,au) companion. The inferred symmetry axis is almost perpendicular to the CO velocity gradient and the orbital plane proposed for the distant ($\sim$50\,au) companion. EP\,Aqr may represent the first misaligned hierarchical triple system with a central AGB star.

## 99. [Forecasting a Cosmological 21 cm Stacking Detection with Early HIRAX Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18981) <a id="link99"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18981

**Authors:** Pascal Hitz, Devin Crichton, Alexandre Refregier

**Abstract:** Post-reionization 21 cm intensity mapping provides a powerful technique for efficiently mapping the Universe on large scales. The Hydrogen Intensity and Real-time Analysis eXperiment (HIRAX) is a compact and highly redundant radio interferometer designed to exploit this technique. We present a forward modelling pipeline for forecasting a statistical detection of the cosmological 21 cm signal with HIRAX through cross-correlation with an external source catalogue. We simulate the cosmological signal using the halo model and propagate it, together with Galactic and extragalactic foreground emission and instrumental noise, through a model of an early-stage 36-dish HIRAX array configuration. We generate simulated visibilities, apply delay-domain foreground filtering, and construct cross-correlation-ready ringmaps for an observing time of 120 days with a single zenith pointing over the frequency range from 700 to 800 MHz, covering approximately 3547 deg$^2$ of the sky. We investigate the prospects for an early statistical signal detection by stacking these ringmaps at the known three-dimensional positions of sources from an overlapping external survey. Specifically, we quantify how delay-domain foreground filtering, the number of stacking sources, catalogue completeness, and source redshift uncertainties affect the detection significance. We find that redshift precision is the most critical requirement: while spectroscopic redshifts with $σ_z / (1+z) \sim 0.001$ preserve a significant stacking signal even under realistic foreground filtering and substantial catalogue incompleteness, photometric redshift uncertainties of $σ_z / (1+z) \sim 0.01$ strongly suppress the signal for realistic delay cutoffs. Applying the framework to a Euclid-like spectroscopic galaxy sample, we recover a minimum signal-to-noise ratio of 11 for our most conservative delay cutoff of 100 ns.

**Abstract:** Characterizing rocky, habitable zone planets in reflected light is a key science objective for future direct imaging missions, and the ultraviolet (UV) is uniquely suited for supporting the characterization of Earth-like atmospheres. The Hartley-Huggins ozone band at $0.25 ~μ\mathrm{m}$ remains detectable at low spectral resolution down to $0.1\%$ PAL $\mathrm{O}_2$, providing a sensitive proxy for oxygen-bearing chemistry even for Proterozoic-like atmospheres where the visible and near-infrared $\mathrm{O}_2$ features become inaccessible. This work uses an end-to-end framework for UV starshade spectroscopy of modern Earth analogs that integrates high-fidelity observation simulation, conservative post-processing, and Bayesian spectral retrieval to quantify ozone detection significance. The framework is applied across 108 simulated scenes spanning a range of planet phase angles, system inclinations, and exozodiacal dust densities. The results establish that robust ozone detection with UV-only, $R=7$ reflected light spectroscopy requires a photometric $\mathrm{SNR} \sim 30$. UV starshade observations with the Habitable Worlds Observatory achieve this threshold within 3-day integrations for solar-type systems at 10 parsecs with inclinations up to $60^\circ$ at favorable phases ($\leq 90^\circ$). Above this SNR threshold, ozone detections are robust to systematic bias from imperfect exozodi calibration within $\sim30\%$, and the median retrieved ozone abundance remains accurate to within one order of magnitude of the true value.

## 101. [The ePESSTO+ census of ZTF nuclear transients. I. What flares in galactic nuclei?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18633) <a id="link101"></a>

**Authors:** E. Concepción, T. Petrushevska, G. Leloudas, D. Ilić, M. Gromadzki, S. Bhattacharyya, P. Charalampopoulos, A. Gomboc, N. Ihanec, M. Nicholl, P. J. Pessi, D. R. Young, P. Wiseman, T. -W. Chen, M. Dennefeld, Y. Dgany, C. P. Gutiérrez, C. Inserra, L. Makrygianni, T. E. Müller-Bravo, S. Nissen, F. Onori, R. Roy, M. Quilt

**Abstract:** Transients in galactic nuclei arise from distinct processes, only some of which trace the supermassive black hole (SMBH). These include tidal disruption events (TDEs), changing-look active galactic nuclei, Bowen fluorescence flares (BFFs) and ambiguous nuclear transients, alongside supernovae that appear nuclear but are unrelated to the SMBH. Reliable classification requires spectroscopy, which will not scale to the alert volumes of the Legacy Survey of Space and Time (LSST). We aim to characterise the population of nuclear transients, to identify early observables capable of distinguishing SMBH-related flares, and TDEs in particular, from the dominant supernova background, and to assess the biases this selection introduces. We implemented a real-time filter on the Lasair broker, selecting transients from the Zwicky Transient Facility (ZTF) alert stream by nuclear coincidence with the host galaxy, with no photometric cuts, to retain SMBH-related flares in both quiescent and active hosts. The filter ran over 16 months, with candidates reported for classification through ePESSTO+ and other facilities. The filter returned 476 candidates, of which 202 were spectroscopically classified, yielding predominantly supernovae, 10 confirmed TDEs (5%) and one BFF. The mean g-r colour and the rise time separate TDEs from the supernova background. Our filter recovers 10 of the 25 TDEs classified in the same period and declination range; most misses are technical or observational rather than physical. Simple real-time filtering combined with basic photometric criteria can already isolate TDEs efficiently from nuclear transient alert streams, and high completeness is achievable. In the LSST era, where samples will be predominantly photometric, selection bias will become as important as contamination, and retaining flares in active hosts is necessary if unusual events are not to be excluded.

These observational findings challenge our understanding of the past evolution of present-day supermassive black holes, and provide novel anchor points to constrain theoretical models of black hole formation and growth. This review summarises our current understanding of the observed properties of newly discovered, active black holes in the early Universe, discusses the status of mass measurements and the relation to their hosts, as well as open questions and future prospects.

## 103. [MRI Turbulence versus $α$-Viscosity in Circumbinary Disks: Implications for Gravitational-Wave Dephasing and Accretion Variability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17212) <a id="link103"></a>

**Authors:** Mudit Garg, Yan-Fei Jiang

**Abstract:** Our understanding of gas torques on massive black hole binaries (MBHBs) relies on hydrodynamical (HD) simulations of circumbinary disks (CBDs) in which an $α$-viscosity models angular momentum transport. However, CBDs around MBHBs near merger are hot, magnetized plasmas where transport is driven by magnetohydrodynamical (MHD) turbulence sustained by the magnetorotational instability (MRI). We test how well an $α$-viscosity captures MRI turbulence with 3D global Athena++ simulations of a locally isothermal CBD around an equal-mass, circular MBHB, run in HD and MHD from the same initial torus on identical grids, so that only the transport mechanism differs. Excising the region within twice the binary separation, $r<2~a_{\rm bin}$, allows $1000$ orbits of evolution, several times longer than previous MHD simulations. Both disks deliver the same accretion rate, and the gas beyond $2~a_{\rm bin}$ exerts a positive torque in both, yet the MHD torque is $3.5$ times larger and $3$ times more variable. Near the midplane, transport is similar in the disk body but three times weaker in MHD at the cavity edge, where $2$-$3$ times more mass piles up in a more eccentric cavity, raising the torque. The accretion rate is modulated mainly at twice the binary frequency in HD, but by a persistent lump at the cavity edge and its beat with the binary in MHD. The outer-disk torque therefore implies a contribution to the gas-induced phase shift in the gravitational-wave signal of LISA binaries $3.5$ times larger than in $α$-disk models, and a different variability signature for LSST and Roman.

**Authors:** Kazuya Iwata, Keiichi Maeda, Ken J. Shen

**Abstract:** One of the key unresolved problems in thermonuclear explosions of white dwarfs (WDs), including type Ia supernovae, is the condition for ignition and propagation of thermonuclear flame. Given the limitation of hydrodynamic simulations in resolving the flame front involved in the explosion process, theoretical considerations for these criteria merit accurate prediction of the onset of deflagration and detonation (deflagration-to-detonation transition or direct initiation) and subsequent stable propagation. Application of cell-based theories, which are originally designed for terrestrial detonation incorporating the size of the cellular structure, to pure-helium detonation in a WD envelope was proven to be powerful. In the present work, we extend this for detonation in helium-carbon and carbon-oxygen mixtures, both of which are associated with the double-detonation model for type Ia supernovae. Comparing with previous hydrodynamic studies in terms of the minimum initiation energy and critical stellar mass for stable propagation, the agreement is satisfactory. For the helium-carbon detonation, it is theoretically substantiated that even an extremely low-mass WD envelope $\sim 10^{-3}$ M$_{\odot}$ can accommodate stable detonation. For the carbon-oxygen detonation, the oxygen-scale cell width is shown to be a good diagnosing indicator. In addition, it is indicated that direct initiation of the carbon-oxygen detonation is only available for a WD heavier than 0.6 M$_\odot$. The findings of this study enable first-order calibration of the explosion model by a simple calculation through the analogy between astrophysical and terrestrial burning, the latter of which can be tested and observed in a laboratory-scale experiment.

**Novelty:** 5

## 105. [The Second Catalog of Low-Energy Fermi-LAT Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18972) <a id="link105"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18972

**Authors:** The Fermi-LAT Collaboration

**Abstract:** We used 14 years of Fermi-Large Area Telescope (LAT) data to derive a catalog of sources detected between 20 MeV and 200 MeV. For each source we provide localization, spectral, and association information. The Second Catalog of Low-Energy Fermi-LAT sources (2FLE) contains 450 sources detected at $TS>25$, with median energy flux of $F_{20-200 \, \text{MeV}} = 1.9\times10^{-11}$ erg cm$^{-2}$ s$^{-1}$. Using a Bayesian and likelihood-ratio method, we associate 377 2FLE sources with a known counterpart. Of the 325 objects associated with extragalactic sources, the dominant source class represented in the 2FLE are flat-spectrum radio quasars (FSRQ). Additionally, 52 sources are associated with Galactic sources. Three associated sources have not been previously detected in any $γ$-ray catalog and represent new $γ$-ray sources of known origin. We find that 387 2FLE sources have a positional cross-match with the 4FGL-DR4 sources. For the sources with a 4FGL counterpart, we compare the spectrum of the 2FLE source to the 4FGL spectral energy distributions. Of the 73 2FLE sources without multi-wavelength associations, 13 have a 4FGL counterpart that lacks classification, while 60 are newly discovered $γ$-ray sources. Among these 60 sources, 51 are found at high latitudes ($|b|>10^{\circ}$), and 38 are detected at $>10σ$. Additionally, we find that out of the 450 2FLE sources 45 are TeV emitters, and 6 are spatially coincident with a 1LHAASO source. The 2FLE helps us pave the way for reducing the ``MeV gap", and provides the most sensitive catalog between 20-200 MeV to date.

## 106. [Dark Matter Stability from residual flavour symmetries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17210) <a id="link106"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17210

**Authors:** Giacomo Landini, Simone Marciano, Federico Mescia

**Abstract:** We propose a model-independent framework in which Dark Matter stability emerges naturally from residual discrete $\mathbb{Z}_N$ symmetries left unbroken by the spontaneous breaking of continuous flavour symmetries, both Abelian and non-Abelian. No ad hoc stabilizing symmetry is required, as Dark Matter stability is an inherent consequence of the underlying flavour dynamics. As an explicit example, we construct a prototype model based on a $\text{SU}(2)_\text{F} \otimes \text{U}(1)_\text{F}$ Froggatt-Nielsen symmetry, which successfully reproduces the observed charged lepton mass spectrum and the PMNS mixing matrix, while identifying the lightest sterile neutrino as a stable Dark Matter candidate. Furthermore, we study the Dark Matter phenomenology within our model in the freeze-in regime, including production channels mediated by higher-dimensional operators suppressed by the cutoff scale $Λ$. Our analysis shows that continuous flavour symmetries can provide a unified, self-consistent framework addressing both the flavour puzzle and the origin, stability, and production of Dark Matter.

## 107. [Quasinormal modes of rotating black holes with ultralight scalar fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16054) <a id="link107"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16054

**Authors:** Leif Lui, Adrian Ka-Wai Chung

**Abstract:** We report the first accurate computation of the leading-order shifts in the quasinormal-mode frequencies of rapidly rotating black holes surrounded by massive scalar fields with nonminimal curvature coupling. For fields with reduced Compton wavelengths no shorter than the black-hole mass scale, the numerical errors in the frequency shifts are typically $10^{-8} - 10^{-5}$ for dimensionless spins between 0 and 0.9, and at most $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$ next to the scalar-mass threshold. Our results open up the possibility of searching for ultralight scalar particles, prime dark-matter candidates, using existing gravitational-wave signals.

## 108. [EMRIs in Kerr Spacetime: The Role of Inclination Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18964) <a id="link108"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18964

**Authors:** Yael Alush, Nicholas C. Stone, Scott A. Hughes

**Abstract:** Extreme mass ratio inspirals (EMRIs) are expected to be a major source of gravitational waves (GWs) for the future {\it Laser Interferometer Space Antenna} ({\it LISA}). An EMRI occurs when a compact object, driven by the emission of gravitational radiation, slowly inspirals into a massive black hole (MBH). The classical formation channel involves repeated two-body interactions within dense nuclear star clusters. However, both EMRI event rates and the expected distribution of their orbital parameters remain highly uncertain. A major source of this uncertainty is the poorly constrained ratio between gradually inspiraling EMRIs and direct plunges, which do not produce detectable GWs. While most studies of astrophysical EMRI production focus on the Schwarzschild limit, previous work examining the Kerr metric found that the spin of the central MBH can significantly affect the EMRI-to-plunge ratio. This enhancement depends sensitively on orbital inclination, yet earlier studies held inclination fixed. In this work, we develop the mathematical framework describing inclination evolution driven by two-body encounters evolving along Kerr geodesics. By implementing 3D diffusion into Monte Carlo simulations, we show that initially prograde orbits may evolve into retrograde states, thereby converting potential EMRIs into plunges or vice versa. We estimate the EMRI-to-plunge ratio and find that substantial MBH spin enhances the EMRI rate by a factor of $\sim 2.5$. Additionally, even for a non-spinning MBH, modeling the orbits as relativistic geodesics rather than Newtonian trajectories increases the number of EMRIs by a similar factor of $\sim 2.5$. The distribution of Kerr EMRI inclinations has a substantial prograde bias that may aid in identifying the subset of loss cone EMRIs within large future EMRI samples.

**Novelty:** 5

## 109. [Twisted Pseudodisk and Asymmetric Mass Accretion on the Circumstellar Disk II: Parameter Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19297) <a id="link109"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19297

**Authors:** Masahiro N. Machida, Shingo Hirano, Shantanu Basu

**Abstract:** Using three-dimensional magnetohydrodynamic simulations accounting for ohmic dissipation, we investigate the long-term evolution of the infalling envelope for $\sim 100,000$\,yr after protostar formation. The primary control parameter is the initial misalignment angle $θ_0$ between the rotation axis and the magnetic field, varied from $0^\circ$ to $90^\circ$. In all misaligned models ($θ_0 \ne 0^\circ$), the pseudodisk (or infalling envelope) surrounding the circumstellar disk develops a highly distorted and complex morphology. Gas accretion from the envelope proceeds through discrete, channel-like flows, producing asymmetric streams rather than axisymmetric infall. Protostellar outflows are launched even in these complex environments, and inflow and outflow regions are strongly interwoven. Outflows transport angular momentum anisotropically, leading to time-dependent changes in disk orientation and outflow direction, which further enhance envelope asymmetry. We examine the relative orientation between gas flows and magnetic fields and find that, although statistical tendencies exist, accretion streams cannot be uniquely characterized as either parallel or perpendicular to the magnetic field, particularly in strongly misaligned cases. We also show that most infalling gas accretes at nearly the free-fall velocity, with only a minor fraction significantly slowed within the pseudodisk. Asymmetric accretion and stream-like structures persist beyond 10,000\,au, demonstrating that large-scale asymmetry can arise intrinsically. Our results show that complex streams and asymmetric envelopes naturally emerge from magnetized core physics, even in the absence of initial turbulence or external gas accretion.

## 110. [An Intermediate-orbit Brown Dwarf Confirmed with Astrometry+Radial Velocity Joint Analysis, and the Eccentricity Distribution of Brown Dwarfs Across Different Locations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16662) <a id="link110"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16662

**Authors:** Lei Ren, Tianjun Gan, Yifan Xuan, Shude Mao, Shigeru Ida, Guang-yao Xiao, Fabo Feng, Enric Pallé

**Abstract:** Intermediate-orbit ($\sim 1$ AU) brown dwarfs with absolute mass measurements are essential for understanding the evolution of substellar objects. Although such systems remain rare, their number is now being enlarged thanks to astrometry. Here, we present the absolute mass measurements for HD 203771B, a massive brown dwarf orbiting a K dwarf ($M_\ast=0.79\pm0.08\ M_\odot$) with a period of 1140 days on an eccentric ($e\approx0.5$) and inclined ($i\approx 130^{\circ}$) orbit. Through joint analysis of (1) radial velocities and Gaia two-body solution, as well as (2) radial velocities, Hipparcos Intermediate Astrometric Data and two-epoch Gaia astrometry, we obtain true companion masses of $74.20^{+3.46}_{-3.64} M_{\mathrm{J}}$ and $69.03^{+4.99}_{-4.95} M_{\mathrm{J}}$, respectively, agreeing with each other within $1σ$. Taking a sample of 159 brown dwarfs with absolute mass measurements from different detection techniques, we investigate their eccentricity distributions across different semi-major axis ranges by fitting a Beta distribution. We find that the peak of eccentricity distribution tends to gradually shift toward lower values from wide orbits ($a>20$ AU) to intermediate ($1<a<20$ AU) and inner ($0.1<a<1$ AU) regions. Such trend is plausibly due to disk friction during orbital evolution. Together with obliquity studies showing that brown dwarfs are mostly aligned, our result suggests that brown dwarfs may migrate inward through quiescent coplanar disk-driven processes.

## 111. [Tracing the Influence of Distant companions on Exoplanetary Systems (TIDES) I. Highly Eccentric and Misaligned Binary Companions to Hot Jupiters](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17811) <a id="link111"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17811

**Authors:** Marvin Morgan, Jerry W. Xuan, Brendan P. Bowler, Kyle Franson, Michael C. Liu, Konstantin Batygin, Heather A. Knutson, Björn Benneke, Julianne Cronin, Luke Finnerty, Chih-Chun Hsu, Aniket Sanghi

**Abstract:** The origin of hot Jupiters remains an open question. A significant fraction reside in binary or multiple star systems, suggesting that stellar companions may play an important role in shaping their present-day architectures. However, the orbits of the vast majority of these stellar companions remain poorly constrained, leaving their role in hot Jupiter evolution an open question. We present the Tracing the Influence of Distant companions on Exoplanetary Systems (TIDES) survey, a campaign to map the orbits of stellar companions in systems hosting hot Jupiters to assess the extent to which binaries play a role in hot Jupiter formation and migration. Here, we introduce our survey design and new orbital fits for the first six systems in our sample---$τ$ Boo, WASP-2, WASP-11, WASP-76, HAT-P-14, and HAT-P-16---by combining previous observations of the stellar companions together with new Keck/NIRC2 imaging and Keck/KPIC relative radial velocities. Among these six systems, the four with the most precisely constrained orbits exhibit high eccentricities ($e \geq 0.8$), and three of these systems also display significant planet--binary misalignments ($Δi \geq 20^\circ$). This suggests the orbital properties of stellar companions to hot Jupiter host stars are qualitatively different than field binaries and binaries hosting inner transiting small planets. These orbital architectures are consistent with stellar companions shaping the evolution of hot Jupiters, either through high-eccentricity migration or by participating in an earlier episode of dynamical evolution that ultimately led to hot Jupiter formation. Continued orbital monitoring of the full sample will reveal whether outer stellar companions play a causal role in the evolution of hot Jupiters.

## 112. [Signature of GW from the Hidden Sector in light of the Recent LZ Event](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18234) <a id="link112"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18234

**Authors:** Sujit Kumar Sahoo, Narendra Sahu, Vicky Singh Thounaojam

**Abstract:** The LZ collaboration has recently reported a single nuclear-recoil event at $E_R=248\pm23_\text{stat}\pm23_\text{sys}$ keV, observed in a 2.84 tonne-year exposure with a local significance of $3.4σ$, pointing towards an inelastic dark matter (DM) interpretation. We explain this event within a gauged $U(1)_X$ hidden-sector model, spontaneously broken by a singlet scalar whose vacuum expectation value (VEV) gives rise to: \textit{(i)} gauge boson mass, \textit{(ii)} a small Majorana mass for the vector-like singlet fermion, the DM candidate, charged under the new symmetry, which splits the fermion into two pseudo-Dirac states (mass splitting proportional to the scalar VEV) thus resulting in a purely off-diagonal vector current, \textit{(iii)} the frequency and the amplitude of the GW signal arising from the hidden sector symmetry breaking via first-order phase transition (FOPT). The symmetry-breaking scale thus provides a multi-messenger probe connecting terrestrial direct-detection kinematics at the LZ and early-universe FOPT thermodynamics while obtaining thermal DM relic abundance. We delineate the viable parameter space compatible with the LZ event, correct relic density, and detectable GW signals in the low frequency regimes at future interferometers like $μ{\rm ARES}$ and THEIA while satisfying existing cosmological, astrophysical, and terrestrial bounds.

## 113. [DEMOS I. A Bayesian inference framework to constrain disc evolution from observed demographics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17254) <a id="link113"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17254

**Authors:** Lorenzo A. Malanga, Giovanni P. Rosotti, Giuseppe Lodato, Benoît Tabone, Alice Somigliana, Leonardo Testi

**Abstract:** The mechanism driving the evolution and dispersal of protoplanetary discs has a substantial impact on the theories of planet formation. To date, neither the viscous paradigm nor the MHD wind-driven framework have been ruled out by the observations. The statistical richness of the observed samples provides a unique opportunity to test evolutionary models through population synthesis. However, comparisons between models and observations are normally carried out by eye, and a more statistically rigorous approach has not yet been implemented. We present DEMOS, a Bayesian inference tool that aims to infer the model parameters and initial conditions that best reproduce the full distribution of global properties of disc populations. This framework relies on a comparison between observations and synthetic populations generated with the 1D disc population-synthesis code Diskpop. We tested the tool with both the MHD wind model and the viscous framework with internal photoevaporation. Applying DEMOS to synthetic populations, we find that, for both models, it accurately recovers the model parameters of disc populations and their associated uncertainties, including cases in which initial conditions are correlated with the stellar mass. In the optimal scenario in which disc masses, accretion rates and disc sizes are available, the tool can also constrain the angular-momentum transport parameter. We show that, although parameter estimates are the most precise with samples of about 100 discs, the tool also yields meaningful constraints for smaller populations of 10-30 discs. We further find that the predictions obtained with DEMOS closely align with those found by previous works. We anticipate that DEMOS will be a valuable tool to infer the initial conditions of observed disc populations, to design observational programmes, and to assess which evolutionary model best reproduces the data.

## 114. [CosmoForge II. An exact treatment of arbitrary linear filters in quadratic maximum-likelihood power spectrum estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18012) <a id="link114"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18012

**Authors:** Giacomo Galloni

**Abstract:** Ground-based cosmic microwave background (CMB) experiments filter their time-ordered data to remove $1/f$ noise and ground pickup, which removes or mixes the large angular scales where primordial $B$ modes peak. A common remedy is a pseudo-$C_\ell$ estimator with harmonic $B$-purification and a transfer function measured from simulations. Another applies it after a matrix-based $E/B$ purification of the maps. We extend the quadratic maximum-likelihood (QML) estimator of CosmoForge to filtered maps, treating the filter exactly: a linear filter conjugates the estimator's ingredients without altering its structure. The same holds for the pixel-based likelihood of CosmoForge, which gives the smallest error bar achievable on the same maps. We compare all methods in a simplified setup under several map-level filters through the error bars on bandpowers and on the tensor-to-scalar ratio $r$. QML sits $2$ to $4\%$ above the pixel-likelihood bound on $σ(r)$ in every filter state. At low noise, the pure pseudo-$C_\ell$ $σ(r)$ is $1.21$ times QML's without filtering, $3.0$ times under a scan filter along stripes of constant declination, and $3.7$ times under one from a toy constant-elevation scan. The matrix-purified pseudo-$C_\ell$ $σ(r)$ is at most $1.03$ times QML's except under the constant-elevation filters, where it reaches $1.10$. These factors shrink towards unity at higher noise. The pure pseudo-$C_\ell$ gap comes from apodisation and from harmonic $E/B$ separation; pixel-space separation closes it, except under the constant-elevation filters, which attenuate modes rather than remove them and widen the gap. QML stays within a few per cent of the optimal error bars without the tuning and simulated transfer functions of the pseudo-$C_\ell$ pipelines. Thus, CosmoForge provides a robust framework for power-spectrum estimation and inference on filtered maps. (Abridged)

## 115. [Seeing Double: The Theory Defying Twin Flares of TDE 2022dbl](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18840) <a id="link115"></a>

**Authors:** Xinze Guo, Ryan Chornock, Raffaella Margutti, Yuhan Yao, Erica Hammerstein, Natalie LeBaron, S. Bradley Cenko, Matthew J. Graham, Dina Ibrahimzade, Itai Sfaradi, Jean Somalwar, Robert Stein, Eli Wiston, Joahan Castaneda Jaimes, Mansi M. Kasliwal, Russ R. Laher

**Abstract:** Partial tidal disruption events, in which a star survives a close passage by a supermassive black hole and may be disrupted again on a subsequent encounter, provide a unique probe of supermassive black hole properties and accretion physics. We examine TDE 2022dbl, a partial tidal disruption event candidate that exhibits two separate UV/optical flares, using 11 new optical spectra, updated UV/optical photometry, and a Keck-II/ESI host-galaxy spectrum. We trace the evolution of the prominent blended N III + He II feature across both flares and find that the blend evolves from N III-dominated to He II-dominated emission, which may reflect changing ionization conditions and a declining efficiency of Bowen fluorescence. The two flares show similar thermal evolution and spectral features, supporting a partial-disruption scenario. The second flare exhibits a broader, plateau-like peak, a longer decline timescale, and less extreme line velocities than the first flare. We suggest that these differences arise from the interaction between debris from the second disruption and an accretion disk formed during the first flare. From the host-galaxy velocity dispersion, we infer a black hole mass of $\log_{10}(M_{\rm BH}/M_\odot)=6.01\pm0.45$. No third flare has been detected at the expected recurrence epoch, leading us to propose a new interpretation in which a positive-energy kick during ballistic core reformation delays the return of the surviving remnant.

This dipole anisotropy is thought to be caused by our local observer motion, however, it is observed to be about three times larger than predicted, with significances exceeding 5$σ$. The SKA is in a unique position to shed light on this anomaly by measuring this and other effects related to our observer motion. To quantify this, we forecast the capability of large-angle HI and continuum surveys of the SKA to constrain the kinematic matter dipole in galaxy number counts, the modulation of the matter power spectrum by a kinematic dipole, and kinematic spectral distortions of diffuse radio emission.

**Authors:** Bikram Keshari Pradhan, Jérôme Margueron, Guy Chanfray, Hubert Hansen

**Abstract:** The question of the link between the nucleon properties and the equation of state of ultra-dense matter, both being composed of quarks and gluons, is investigated by adopting a soliton model to describe internal nucleon properties and a direct mapping between nucleon and quark matter to obtain the equation of state of ultra-dense matter. We compare $π+ω$ and $π+ω+ρ$ soliton models and analyze the presence of a pressure singularity at the nucleon center due to the $ρ$ field. This singularity impacts the equation of state in ultra-dense matter. Only the pressure derived from the canonical energy-momentum tensor has a singularity, while the Belinfante pseudo-gauge transformation of the energy-momentum tensor generates an internal pressure without a singularity. We then investigate predictions for the equation of state of ultra-dense matter based on different prescriptions for the nucleon pressure. It is shown that none of the prescriptions agree with the thermodynamical pressure defined in uniform matter. The mismatch between any equation of state and the thermodynamical one questions the simplicity of the direct mapping between finite-size nucleon properties and uniform matter as well as the role of the nucleon surface in the internal energy density and the pressure.

**Authors:** Qi-Lin Yang, Shi-Jie Wang, Nan Li

**Abstract:** The cosmic reheating is an important epoch in the very early Universe, and has significant impacts on the primordial black hole (PBH) abundance and the scalar-induced gravitational wave (SIGW) spectrum. In this work, we perform a full parameter space Bayesian inference, using the NANOGrav 15-yr dataset to constrain the thermal history of reheating. Current pulsar timing array data can effectively constrain the peak frequency of the SIGW spectrum as $f_{\rm p}\sim 10^{-7.6}$--$10^{-7.0}$ Hz, along with $f_{\rm reh}\sim 10^{-9.0}$--$10^{-8.3}$ Hz and $w\sim 0.19$--$0.79$ (the $68\%$ confidence level), when the power spectrum has a relatively narrow width $Δ\lesssim 1$. A broader power spectrum introduces a smoothing effect that obscures the characteristic frequency knee in the SIGW spectrum, leading to the loss of information about cosmic reheating. Furthermore, for the Dirac $δ$ and narrow spectra, the PBH masses associated with the SIGWs are anchored at the planetary mass scale around $10^{-6}$--$10^{-4} M_{\odot}$. ## 121. [Understanding the Complex Morphology of a Coronal Mass Ejection. III: How to Create a Severe Geomagnetic Storm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19096) <a id="link121"></a>

**Authors:** Cecilia Mac Cormack, Abril Sahade, Erika Palmerio, Evangelia Samara, Phillip Hess, Robin C. Colaninno, Teresa Nieves-Chinchilla

**Abstract:** On April 21, 2023, a complex filament eruption produced an Earth-directed coronal mass ejection (CME) that triggered the first severe geomagnetic storm of solar cycle 25. The primary objective of this work is to understand the physical origin of the severe impact of this CME on Earth. We explore the CME's complex morphology by simulating the eruption using the thermodynamic magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) model CORHEL-CME. We model the pre-eruptive structure as two intertwined magnetic flux ropes and validate the early evolution of the event by comparing synthetic observables generated from the model with extreme-ultraviolet and white-light observations. We then compare the simulated CME structure with in situ measurements. From our analysis, we conclude that the CME merged with a stream interaction region (SIR) before reaching Earth. This interaction compressed the CME sheath, producing a sustained negative $B_n$ component that, together with the associated enhancement in solar wind density and velocity, triggered the geomagnetic storm. The internal configuration of the magnetic flux rope, which also maintained a sustained southward component during its propagation toward Earth, intensified the storm to severe levels. This model-data comparison underscores the power of using MHD simulations, when validated by multi-viewpoint observations, to reveal hidden dynamical processes and improve the understanding and prediction of space weather impacts.

## 122. [Broadband non-thermal emission predictions based on 3D simulations of supernova explosions within active galactic nuclei jets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18476) <a id="link122"></a>

**Authors:** B. Longo, V. Bosch-Ramon, M. Perucho, J. M. Martí

**Abstract:** Thermonuclear and core-collapse supernovae are statistically expected to happen within extragalactic jets, as these jets cross the inner regions of their host galaxies, and supernova ejecta-jet interactions can potentially lead to strong non-thermal activity. We model the non-thermal emission from a supernova interacting with a jet on kiloparsec scales, using detailed numerical simulations of the interaction. For intermediate jet powers, radio, optical and X-ray fluxes from supernova ejecta-jet interactions may be detectable for distances < 100Mpc. The non-thermal luminosity budget and local conditions associated with these interactions could be enough to explain knots seen in kiloparsec scale jets at different energies. These conditions might also be suitable for the production of heavy ultra high-energy cosmic rays, but photon and neutrino generation from hadronic processes would be minor on kiloparsec scales. The leptonic gamma-ray fluxes are modest but might non-negligibly contribute in TeV for the nearest or more powerful jet sources.

## 123. [Two sides of the same spin: tracking optical and radio pulsations of SAX J1808.4-3658 from outburst to quiescence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18322) <a id="link123"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18322

**Authors:** C. Ballocco, A. Miraval Zanon, A. Papitto, M. Burgay, M. C. Baglio, C. Malacaria, F. Ambrosino, A. Possenti, G. Illiano, E. D. Barr, R. P. Breton, L. Burderi, S. Campana, C. J. Clark, F. Coti Zelati, D. de Martino, T. Di Salvo, M. Kramer, R. La Placa, F. Lewis, A. Marino, D. M. Russell, A. Sanna, L. Stella, B. Stappers, J. D. Turner, M. Cecconi, A. Ghedina

**Abstract:** High-time-resolution optical and UV observations have revealed bright coherent pulsations from accreting millisecond pulsars, exceeding the luminosity expected from thermal emission from NS hotspots or X-ray reflection by the accretion disk. Characterizing optical pulsations over a wide range of accretion rates through coordinated multi-wavelength observations can shed light on their origin. We present a timing study of the AMSP SAX J1808.4-3658 during its 2022 outburst and quiescence, using optical TNG/SiFAP2 observations, simultaneous NICER X-ray data, LCO optical monitoring, and MeerKAT radio observations in quiescence. During the outburst, we detect coherent optical pulsations with pulsed luminosities of 1.3x10^(30)erg/s. Combined with previous detections, our results suggest that the optical pulsed amplitude increases as the X-ray flux decreases. The X-ray and optical pulsed luminosities show opposite trends with X-ray luminosity, suggesting different responses to changes in the accretion rate. We measure X-ray-to-optical delays of 0.88+-0.04ms and 0.51+-0.03 ms in two outburst observations, indicating that the relative timing between the signals is not constant. Together with the spectral energy distribution, these results support different emission mechanisms: non-thermal emission from relativistic particles for optical pulsations and channeled accretion onto the NS surface for X-ray pulsations. During quiescence, we detect no coherent optical pulsations, obtaining a 95% confidence upper limit of <2.2x10^(29)erg/s on the optical pulsed luminosity, corresponding to an optical efficiency comparable to those of spider radio millisecond pulsars. Simultaneous MeerKAT observations reveal no radio pulsations down to ~10μJy, significantly improving previous constraints but not excluding an active rotation-powered pulsar whose radio emission may be absorbed by intra-binary material.

## 124. [Sensitivity of calibration-free DESI DR2 BAO--supernova constraints to a distance-duality tilt](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16496) <a id="link124"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16496

**Authors:** Mikel Artola, Stéphane Fay, Leandros Perivolaropoulos

**Abstract:** Recent measurements from DESI baryon acoustic oscillations (BAO) have reported a moderate preference for dynamical dark energy, challenging the $Λ$CDM paradigm. Nonetheless, such a trend could reflect a tension between the shape and the offset of the Hubble diagrams predicted by current BAO and Type Ia supernovae (SN) data, which may underpin a possible breakdown of the cosmic distance duality relation linking luminosity distances $D_{\rm L}$ and angular-diameter distances $D_{\rm A}$. We explore this possibility by parametrizing deviations from this relation as $η(z)\equiv D_{\rm L}/[{(1+z)}^2D_{\rm A}]=α{(1+z)}^\varepsilon$, and investigate how the preference for a Chevallier-Polarski-Linder (CPL) dynamical dark energy is modified once $\varepsilon\neq0$ is allowed. Using DESI DR2 and uncalibrated SN data (Pantheon$+$, DES-Dovekie, Union3, and Union3.1), we find that allowing $\varepsilon$ to vary freely in both $Λ$CDM and CPL reduces the CPL preference with respect to $Λ$CDM by a larger amount than the addition of spatial curvature $Ω_k$; only Union3.1 exhibits comparable reductions. We also find that the preference reduction is robust to alternative $η(z)$ parametrizations, and that the $η(z)$ constraints remain stable under the removal of BAO tracers or low-redshift cutoffs for Pantheon$+$, whereas the DES-Dovekie preference largely disappears once $z<0.1$ SN are removed. In a separate calibrated analysis, illustrative Planck sound-horizon and SH0ES absolute-magnitude priors give $α\simeq0.942\pm0.014$, quantifying the normalization mismatch under those assumptions. We also discuss photon-loss and varying-$G$ interpretations: ordinary positive opacity cannot explain the negative central tilts $\varepsilon$ found for a $Λ$CDM background, while local gravity tests strongly constrain the illustrative unscreened varying-$G$ scenario.

## 125. [The nearby Type II supernova 2024ggi beyond 600 days: circumstellar interaction and dust formation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17140) <a id="link125"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17140

**Authors:** L. Ferrari, G. Folatelli, C. P. Gutiérrez, A. Méndez, C. Ashall, K. Medler, K. Ertini, H. Kuncarayakti, N. Morrell, M. Stritzinger, W. Hoogendam, E. Hueichapán, R. Cartier, J. P. Anderson, M. Bersten, L. Galbany, J. L. Prieto, C. Cárdenas Uribe, S. Vazhayil

**Abstract:** The Type II SN 2024ggi is one of the nearest core-collapse SNe to have exploded in recent decades. Its proximity has enabled extensive multiwavelength monitoring from early times through the nebular phase. Signatures of late-time interaction between the SN ejecta and hydrogen-rich circumstellar material (CSM) have recently been reported. Here we extend its study using late-time optical photometry and spectroscopy from 570 to 820 d after explosion. We investigate the emergence and evolution of late-time ejecta-CSM interaction and constrain the geometry of the interaction region and the properties of newly formed dust. Comparisons with other interacting SNe II and with synthetic spectra are presented. We model the H$α$ profile with the Monte Carlo radiative-transfer code Damocles, including both gas and dust components. Spectroscopic signatures of interaction precede the photometric flattening. The spectra evolve from ejecta-dominated to interaction-dominated as H$α$ develops a broad, boxy profile. The optical light curves subsequently flatten relative to the $^{56}$Co decay rate at approximately 650-670 d. The emission lines show a systematic blue-to-red asymmetry that requires a dust component newly formed within the ejecta. The inferred dust mass formed in the ejecta is $10^{-6}-10^{-5}$ M$_\odot$ if the grains are amorphous carbon or a carbon-silicate mixture, but can reach up to $10^{-3}$ M$_\odot$ for pure silicates.

## 126. [IC 1297: The Most Distant Known X-ray Planetary Nebula and One of the Most Luminous](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17741) <a id="link126"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17741

**Authors:** H. Yuan, Q. A. Parker, M. A. Guerrero

**Abstract:** We present the first targeted Einstein Probe (EP) X-ray observations of any planetary nebula (PN). Using the onboard Follow-up X-ray Telescope (FXT), we detect X-ray emission within the central region of IC 1297. The spectral analysis yields a plasma temperature of $kT = 0.086^{+0.054}_{-0.058}$ keV ($T\sim1.0 \times 10^{6}$ K), characteristic of shocked wind bubbles in PNe, whereas there is no evidence that its Wolf-Rayet central star emits X-rays. The absorption-corrected X-ray luminosity in the 0.3--2.0 keV energy band is $L_{\mathrm{X}} = 2.8 \times 10^{32}$ erg s$^{-1}$, also consistent with a hot bubble. At an adopted distance of $\sim3.8$ kpc, IC 1297 is the most distant X-ray emitting PN found to date, opening up a new observational window on hot-bubble formation and evolution. It is also among the most luminous known X-ray-emitting PNe. Our results demonstrate the capability of the EP FXT for detecting and characterizing faint, extended X-ray sources in the soft X-ray band for distant PNe, extending the known spectral properties of detectable X-ray emitting PNe. We also compiled the X-ray properties of all 41 known Galactic XPNe, finding a clear correlation between distance and X-ray luminosity that reflects an observational bias toward detecting the brightest sources at large distances. The compilation also shows a separation in X-ray luminosity between hot bubbles powered by H-rich and [WR]-type central stars, with the latter reaching luminosities up to an order of magnitude higher. The ten most luminous XPNe all host [WR]/PG 1159 central stars.

## 127. [Nonequilibrium framework for first-order phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16385) <a id="link127"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16385

**Authors:** Jie Liu, Ligong Bian

**Abstract:** The first-order phase transition is central to electroweak baryogenesis and gravitational wave generation, yet thermal equilibrium effective potential analyses cannot describe the real-time non-equilibrium dynamics of the phase transition process. We use the Schwinger-Keldysh closed-time-path formalism of real-time finite-temperature field theory to study these effects. For the $φ^6$ model, we derive the effective action and the corresponding Langevin equation. We further extend the formalism to the two scalar fields case, obtaining the coupled Langevin equations. These results provide a theoretical framework for the bubble nucleation simulation of the non-equilibrium phase transition process, laying the foundation for reliable predictions of baryogenesis and gravitational wave signals.

## 128. [Studying the Near-horizon Jet of M87 using Millimeter Balloon-borne VLBI stations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19204) <a id="link128"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.19204

**Authors:** Felix M. Thiel, Dominic W. Pesce, Mayukh Bagchi, Laura M. Fissel, Lindy Blackburn

**Abstract:** In this paper we present synthetic Very Long Baseline Interferometry (VLBI) observations of M87 between a near-future configuration of the Event Horizon Telescope (EHT) and one or multiple 2.5 m-class balloon-borne VLBI stations on a northern transatlantic flight trajectory launching from Kiruna, Sweden. These balloon-borne VLBI stations have the advantage of operating above 99.5% of the atmosphere and their motion allows for efficient sampling of the uv-plane. Assuming frequency phase transfer between 230 and 86 GHz we run these simulations using a modified version of the ngehtsim software package. Our simulations demonstrate the ability of such a VLBI array to probe M87 at the jet base adding baselines down to 0.2 G$λ$ for a single balloon-VLBI station and 0.07 G$λ$ for three balloon VLBI stations. Using the Bayesian imaging software Comrade.jl, we demonstrate the recovery of jet structures up to 150 $μ$as in size for a single balloon and up to 300 $μ$as in size with three balloons. Finally, we test the improvement in jet image fidelity by computing the normalized cross-correlation (NXCOR) and normalized-root-mean-square error (NRMSE) between the image reconstruction and ground truth. We find a factor of 1.2 improvement in the jet NRMSE for a single balloon and a factor of 1.3 improvement for 3 balloons as compared to EHT-only simulations.

## 129. [Extrapolating Collisional Rate Coefficients for Molecules of Astrophysical Interest Using Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18278) <a id="link129"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18278

**Authors:** Yigit Ergen, Jérôme Loreau

**Abstract:** State-to-state rate coefficients for inelastic molecular collisions are essential inputs for non-local thermodynamic equilibrium radiative transfer models, yet their computation using quantum scattering methods is prohibitive for large molecules and highly excited rotational states. We investigate the ability of artificial neural networks to extend existing datasets of collisional excitation rate coefficients without performing additional scattering calculations. A multilayer perceptron model was trained using molecular quantum numbers, level energies, energy gaps, and kinetic temperature as descriptors, and optimized on logarithmic rate coefficients. The model was tested on diverse collision systems ranging from simple diatomic molecules (CO, SiO) to open-shell species (SO), small asymmetric tops (H$_2$O, SO$_2$), and complex organic molecules (benzonitrile and vinyl cyanide) colliding with H$_2$ molecules or He atoms. Extrapolations were performed toward higher rotational levels, higher temperatures, and combinations of both. For most systems, predictions of rate coefficients involving previously unseen rotational states yielded mean error factors between 1.1 and 1.5, with the dominant rate coefficients generally reproduced more accurately than weaker transitions. Temperature extrapolations proved particularly successful, while combined extrapolations in temperature and rotational levels remained accurate to within a factor of two in most cases. The prediction quality depends on the interplay between dataset size, temperature range, molecular complexity, and the presence of clear collisional propensity rules. Overall, the approach demonstrates that machine learning can provide a practical route to extending collisional datasets for astrophysical applications, especially for large molecules for which additional quantum-scattering calculations are computationally demanding.

## 130. [Decay-Assisted Leptogenesis during Preheating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17084) <a id="link130"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17084

**Authors:** Wenbin Zhao

**Abstract:** We revisit the non-thermal leptogenesis mechanism during preheating using the quantum Boltzmann equations. This framework allows us to study the production and decay of heavy right-handed neutrinos in a consistent manner. Due to their fermionic nature, right-handed neutrino production can saturate after a few inflaton oscillations when their occupation numbers approach unity. Right-handed neutrino decays can deplete their occupation numbers, allowing more particles to be produced and generating a larger lepton asymmetry. Compared with the case where right-handed neutrinos decay only after production has finished, we find that the final baryon asymmetry can be enhanced by two orders of magnitude.

## 131. [How much can shape degeneracies affect the Hubble constant measurement?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15999) <a id="link131"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.15999

**Authors:** Ashish K. Meena, Prasenjit Saha, Liliya L. R. Williams, Derek Perera

**Abstract:** Cluster lens models are constrained almost entirely by observed lensed image positions, leaving a large space of mass perturbations with arbitrary amplitudes such that they reproduce every image exactly. Such degeneracies are known as shape degeneracies (ShaDes). We show that these ShaDes shift the observed time delays, by the difference of the perturbing potential between the two images, without depending on the number of source/lens planes. Bounding their amplitude by preserving the positivity of the convergence, image multiplicity, and parity, in MACS J0138.0$-$2155, they can contribute at least $\simeq7$ days to the delay between the detected images of SN-Encore (i.e., $17.5\%$ of the measured value of $39.8$ days) and $\simeq112$ days (i.e., $2.8$--$3.7\%$ of predicted value of $3,000-4000$ days) to the reappearance intervals predicted for SN-Encore and SN-Requiem. These error bars transfer one-to-one to $H_0$, and remain invisible in comparisons of independent models.

## 132. [Observational evidence of black hole event horizon from ultra-compact X-ray binaries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17618) <a id="link132"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17618

**Authors:** Qian-Qi Ma, Wei-Min Gu, Shan-Shan Weng, Mouyuan Sun

**Abstract:** The event horizon is the most crucial feature that distinguishes black hole (BH) from all other objects in the universe. However, since no radiation can escape from the event horizon, its direct observational evidence remains lacking. Although both are compact objects, the event horizon leads to differences in the radiative behavior between black hole and neutron star (NS) X-ray binaries. Historically efforts to probe the existence of event horizons revealed that the BH X-ray binaries are fainter than NSs with similar orbital periods in their quiescent states. Here, we use a special class of X-ray binaries namely the ultra-compact X-ray binaries (UCXBs), to reveal the existence of the event horizon near stellar-mass black holes. In short-period UCXBs, gravitational radiation dominates the orbital angular momentum loss, allowing us to accurately determine their accretion rates. Based on five UCXBs with orbital periods shorter than 40 min, located in globular clusters, and identified accretors (NS or BH), we obtained their accretion rates. Combining their accretion rates and X-ray luminosities, we calculated the X-ray radiative efficiencies of NS and BH. The radiative efficiency of BH is about two to three orders of magnitude lower than that of NS systems, indicating the accretion energy is swallowed by the BH, thereby indirectly probe the presence of the event horizon.

**Novelty:** 4

## 133. [OASIS: On-the-fly ASynchronous Image Stream classification -- Transformer-based models for the early detection of strongly lensed supernovae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17168) <a id="link133"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.17168

**Authors:** Satadru Bag, Raoul Canameras, Sherry H. Suyu, Stefan Schuldt, Stefan Taubenberger, Irham Taufik Andika, Alejandra Melo

**Abstract:** Time-domain surveys often produce asynchronous image sequences across irregular observation times and photometric bands. In many applications, predictions are needed after each new observation, particularly when transient sources must be identified early to trigger follow-up. Strongly lensed supernovae (LSNe), for which follow-up is particularly time-critical, provide a useful example. We formulate this setting as On-the-fly ASynchronous Image Stream (OASIS) classification and explore reusable transformer architectures for causal, on-the-fly classification. We demonstrate these architectures on the lensed-transient classification problem studied by Bag et al. (2026a,b), distinguishing LSNe Ia from unlensed astrophysical transients. We compare them with the recurrent baseline adopted by Bag et al. (2026b), which combines a ConvLSTM branch for image processing with a separate LSTM branch for encoding observation times. The baseline maps asynchronous observations onto a fixed band-by-observation grid, filling unobserved bands with zeros. Using a common training setup, we compare two transformer-based approaches to modeling interactions among spatio-temporal tokens: factorized attention, which separates spatial and temporal interactions, and joint attention, which models them together. On the demonstration dataset, factorized architectures achieve mean losses of $0.095$--$0.100$, compared with $0.128$ for the recurrent baseline and $0.107$ or higher for joint architectures. A selective readout further reduces the best loss to $0.089$ for a factorized model. These results suggest that factorized attention suits this dataset better, without implying universal architectural superiority. We release the architectures as reusable foundations for time-domain astronomy and other applications involving evolving spatial features that require on-the-fly predictions.

**Novelty:** 4

**Abstract:** The two 4-m class telescopes at the Devasthal site in India are complementary: the 4-m International Liquid Mirror Telescope (ILMT) is dedicated to the discovery of new transient phenomena, which can be followed up using the 3.6-m Devasthal Optical Telescope (DOT) for their optical characterisation. However, these transient discoveries still require spectroscopic confirmation to determine their types, for which spectra obtained with the DOT are essential. We investigate their photometric and spectroscopic properties using ILMT light curves, supplemented with ZTF data and DOT spectroscopic observations.

## 135. [Establishing baseline model performances for optical turbulence forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18702) <a id="link135"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18702

**Authors:** M. De Sepibus, E. Masciadri, C. Weinberger, A. Otarola, S. Miesske, C. Veillet, S. Ragland

**Abstract:** Accurate forecasting of optical turbulence (OT) is useful to optimise telescope scheduling and maximising observing efficiency, as the performance of adaptive optics systems strongly depends on turbulence conditions. While various numerical weather prediction and machine learning models are developed to predict these complex turbulence properties, properly evaluating their added value requires well-defined baseline reference models. This study establishes baseline performance thresholds for key astroclimatic parameters using two distinct models: one forecasting the parameter average over a specific timescale, and the other forecasting the temporal evolution over the same specific timescale. We apply these frameworks to extensive, multi-year datasets from the sites of the Very Large Telescope (VLT) and the Large Binocular Telescope (LBT) and discuss the possibility of extrapolating reference values of forecast performances for other sites based on their optical turbulence statistical characteristics. Our results show that baseline predictive accuracy varies according to the observing site, the specific parameter, the chosen timescale, and the forecasting approach. Therefore, an OT forecasting model's accuracy cannot be quantified in absolute terms, but relatively to the baseline.

## 136. [Peering into the Midplane of the DM Tau Disk using N2H+ and DCO+](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18821) <a id="link136"></a>

**Authors:** Amina Diop, Kevin Flaherty, Chunhua Qi, Ilse Cleeves, A. Meredith Hughes, Agnes Kospal, Sean Andrews, David J. Wilner, Jacob B. Simon, Deryl E. Long

**Abstract:** Planets form in the midplanes of protoplanetary disks, thus investigating the properties of the midplane region can shed light on the physical and chemical processes governing the evolution of planetary systems. In this work, we study the conditions near the midplane of the disk around DM Tau using 0."3 (~43 au) ALMA observations of N2H+ (J = 3 - 2) and DCO+ (J = 4 - 3). The radial profiles reveal that DCO+ is centrally peaked, with a depression near 120 au, while N2H+ exhibits a central depression and a peak near 100 au. In addition, both molecules display extended emission out to 400 au. Using parametric models to interpret our observations, we note that a fiducial model based on CO-derived parameters captures the features within 200 au, but fails to reproduce the outer disk emission. We explore various mechanisms that could account for this extended emission and conclude that it is best reproduced by an increased external UV penetration and cosmic ray/X-ray ionization rate, possibly enhanced by radial drift. These processes affect the precursors of N2H+ and DCO+ : increased UV leads to the photodesorption of CO and N2, while ionization enhances H3+ abundance. Besides, the observed N2H+ and DCO+ features are broadly consistent with previous CO snowline constraints, but can also be shaped by processes such as ionization, CO depletion, and multiple chemical pathways. Thus, using these molecular ions as robust CO snowline tracers requires additional constraints from complementary molecular tracers and detailed chemical modeling.

Stellar parameters were constrained using radial velocity, proper motions and stellar evolution models. SPICY 30021 is an optically obscured FUor with a total interstellar and circumstellar extinction of Av = 12.5 mag. Its spectrum closely resembles those of classical FUors. Multiple methods yield a distance of 1.7+_0.4 kpc. Its accretion luminosity is Lacc = 32 L_sol, moderate among FUors. Quiescent near-infrared photometry is consistent with a young 0.9 M_sol progenitor, although the mass depends on the adopted model and distance. We confirm SPICY 30021 as a bona fide FUor event. ## 139. [The Thousand-Pulsar-Array programme on MeerKAT - XX. Co-existing interacting periodicities and polarization modulation in PSR J1901-0906](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16571) <a id="link139"></a>

**Authors:** Jui-An Hsu, Patrick Weltevrede, Geoff Wright, Michael J Keith, Aris Karastergiou, Xiaoxi Song

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive analysis of the complex subpulse modulation in PSR J1901-0906 using data from the Thousand-Pulsar-Array (TPA) programme. Our analysis reveals the coexistence of three distinct periodicities in the subpulse modulation of both profile components, which obey a beat relation. We also detect additional spectral beat features caused by the modulation of these co-existing periodicities by investigating the Fourier phase correlation in two-dimensional fluctuation spectra. Moreover, the single-pulse polarization states are shown to be modulated and synchronized with the three co-existing periodicities observed in total intensity. Such synchronization has been reported for only a small number of pulsars, but this discovery marks the only one where this synchronization involves multiple co-existing periodicities. The modulation beats are similar to, yet more complex than, the beat system previously identified in PSR J1514-4834 with higher-order harmonics playing a significant role. The coexistence of the observed beat-like periodicities in PSR J1901-0906 implies that they are not independent, but rather originate from an underlying, interconnected physical system within the pulsar magnetosphere. Building upon the same geometric framework previously applied to PSR J1514-4834, we demonstrate that a feedback system with interacting rotating carousels successfully reproduces the complex subpulse modulations observed in PSR J1901-0906 and naturally explains the beat relations.

## 140. [Aditya-L1 and SDO Observations of a Confined X-Class Flare with an EUV Late Phase: Thermal and Nonthermal Emission, Magnetic Topology, and Flux Rope Confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16551) <a id="link140"></a>

**Abstract:** We study a long-duration, confined X2.0-class solar flare exhibiting three distinct episodes of HXR emission, along with an accompanying EUV late phase. A key highlight of this work is the novel observations from the high-resolution solar X-ray spectrometers SoLEXS and HEL1OS onboard Aditya-L1, providing insights into the evolution of thermal and non-thermal emissions. The first stage corresponds to an M4.6 flare, while the overall event reaches X2.0 on the GOES scale. During the first phase, magnetic reconnection beneath the large EUV hot channel causes plasma heating to $\sim$19~MK, with a non-thermal spectral index of $\sim$4.3. In the second phase, characterized by external reconnection and confinement of the MFR hot channel, the temperature increases to $\sim$22~MK, accompanied by further hardening of the spectral index to $\sim$3.9. Despite pronounced non-thermal signatures and enhanced particle acceleration, the MFR fails to erupt through the overlying coronal loops because of the strong strapping force exerted by the large overlying coronal loops. Importantly, the stalled eruption, together with reconnection in the higher overlying loops, results in enhanced heating within a closed magnetic configuration, with temperatures peaking at up to $\sim$15~MK during the late phase. The paper also provides the first observations of detectable non-thermal X-ray emission during the early EUV late phase, albeit briefly, providing direct evidence for reconnection-induced heating, likely occurring in the higher overlying coronal loops.

## 141. [Deep learning methods for gamma event selection in TAIGA-IACT image analysis in stereo-mode](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16269) <a id="link141"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.16269

**Authors:** E. O. Gres, A. P. Kryukov, P. A. Volchigov, A. P. Demichev, J. J. Dubenskaya, D. P. Zhurov, S. P. Polyakov, E. B. Postnikov, A. J. Razumov

**Abstract:** In astrophysical complex TAIGA there are four Imaging Atmospheric Cherenkov telescopes (IACTs), located at a distance of 300-500 m from each other, due to which extensive air showers (EAS) generated by cosmic and gamma radiation are recorded by a single telescope or by multiple telescopes simultaneously (stereo-mode). The analysis of EAS images from two or more Cherenkov telescopes together is of particular interest, since for this approach the reconstruction of physical parameters of the primary gamma ray is much more accurate than in mono-observations. However, the gamma-ray event reconstruction strongly depends on the quality of the hadron background suppression of the classifier, therefore, the gamma event selection stage is one of the important ones in the further analysis of IACT data. We propose and study several neural network methods for gamma event selection in joint TAIGA-IACT image analysis. Neural network methods we suggested involves a single-stage analysis of IACT images from two telescopes, while the second method considers the formation of autoencoder-derived essential features that contain the EAS physical parameters, including the type of primary particle.

## 142. [Dynamical and thermal evolution of localized reconnecting current sheets under varying guide-fields in the solar corona](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18111) <a id="link142"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18111

**Authors:** Saksham Pande, Abhishekh Kumar Srivastava, Fabio Bacchini, Sripan Mondal, Akash Bairagi, Balveer Singh, Kartika Sangal, Sudheer K. Mishra, Xinping Zhou, Ding Yuan, R. -Y. Kwon, B. N. Dwivedi

**Abstract:** Although magnetic reconnection in coronal current sheets (CSs) is a fundamental energy release mechanism in the solar corona, the role of guide-fields in governing its formation and self-consistent evolution is not fully understood. Through 2.5D resistive magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) simulations, we investigate how guide-fields affect the formation and evolution of a localized reconnecting coronal CS. Strong guide-fields prevent oppositely directed field lines from approaching sufficiently to form a CS, delaying and suppressing its subsequent evolution. The reconnection rate exhibits a rising phase resembling a power-law, with exponent $δ_1$ decreasing with rising guide-fields, indicating slower temporal growth of the reconnection rate. The aspect ratio of the CS follows a similar power-law trend with the exponent $α_1$ decreasing with rising guide-fields. A subsequent slower growth phase of the reconnection rate shows a power-law exponent $δ_2$ that increases with rising guide-fields, as does the corresponding aspect-ratio exponent $α_2$. As the CS evolves, the latter phase is achieved due to a sharp decremental transition of the guide-field within the CS after a time when the increasing inflows are inefficiently resisted by the guide-field itself. In the reconnection regime during prior phases, the power-law exponents of the maximum temperature and the outflow velocities also decrease with rising guide-fields demonstrating that stronger guide-fields suppress the growth of these indicators also. This study suggests that a self-consistently formed CS displays phases of efficient and inefficient quenching of the reconnection mechanism, promoted by non-zero guide-fields. We also quantify this behavior for indicators of the reconnection process and discuss their applicability to observations of apparent CSs and probing background coronal conditions.

## 143. [Radio ghosts: Radio polarimetry study of newly discovered cluster minihalo candidates or radio tailed galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18099) <a id="link143"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2610.18099

**Authors:** Sam Taziaux, Christopher J. Riseley, Stefan W. Duchesne, Francesca Loi, Alec J. M. Thomson, Aritra Basu, Timothy J. Galvin, Dominik J. Bomans

**Abstract:** Extended diffuse radio emission in the central regions of galaxy clusters is commonly associated with non-thermal processes in the intracluster medium (ICM), but its origin and the mechanisms responsible for sustaining it remain uncertain. We present the discovery and multi-wavelength analysis of two diffuse radio sources, MKTJ0355-4237 and MKTJ0355-4230, to assess their nature and investigate their radio spectral and magnetic field properties through radio polarimetric analysis. We use deep MeerKAT L-band (1.28 GHz) observations to perform spectral analysis of the diffuse emission and study its polarisation properties via rotation measure synthesis. These are complemented by eROSITA X-ray data and DESI photometric redshift catalogues to characterise the host cluster environments. MKTJ0355-4237 is confirmed as cluster-scale minihalo with extent of $\sim 400$ kpc, while the other diffuse source MKTJ0355-4230 remain uncertain and could be either associated with a radio minihalo or radio tailed galaxy. Both show steep spectra ($α\approx -0.7$ to $-0.8$) that steepen toward the outskirts ($α\lesssim -2.5$), consistent with spatial variations in electron ageing and re-acceleration. Polarised emission is detected in both systems, with fractional polarisation $\sim1\,\%$ in the centres and increasing in the outer regions of MKTJ0355-4237. MKTJ0355-4230 is associated with a previously uncatalogued cluster and shows an X-ray morphology offset from the brightest cluster galaxy, indicative of dynamical activity. MKTJ0355-4237 exhibits a sharply bounded internal radio deficit without a corresponding X-ray counterpart, within current sensitivity limits. The spectral steepening supports turbulent re-acceleration as the origin of the diffuse emission.
